With the fix propagated through the v0.57.0 image, all four stubs tick cleanly through the rule-based predict path and the operator can validate the executor end-to-end on signal patterns the existing `BtcLogRegEVSFD` (which sits HOLD silently) does not exercise. Limen v3.0.6 pin unchanged

## v0.58.0 on 10th of May, 2026

- Add `translate_order_side`, `translate_order_type`, `translate_execution_mode`, `translate_maker_preference`, and `translate_stp_mode` helpers to [`praxis/command_translator.py`](praxis/command_translator.py). Each helper re-keys a foreign enum member to the matching Praxis [`praxis.core.domain.enums`](praxis/core/domain/enums.py) member by `.value`, raising `TypeError` if the input lacks a string `.value` and `ValueError` if the value has no Praxis equivalent. Praxis members pass through identity-equal so callers that already hold Praxis enums pay no overhead
- Wire the new translators into [`launcher.py`](praxis/launcher.py)'s `submit_command_with_translated_params` so every Nexus `TradeCommand` enum (`side`, `order_type`, `execution_mode`, `maker_preference`, `stp_mode`) is rebuilt as the Praxis enum member before the call reaches `Trading.submit_command`. Without this, `validate_trade_command` (called inside `ExecutionManager.submit_command`) rejected every order with `no allowed order types configured for mode SINGLE_SHOT` because `_ALLOWED_ORDER_TYPES.get(cmd.execution_mode)` returned `None` — the dict is keyed by Praxis `ExecutionMode` members but `cmd.execution_mode` was a Nexus `ExecutionMode` instance with the same `.value` but a different class identity. The same hidden-class-mismatch trap silently bypassed `TradeCommand.__post_init__`'s `self.execution_mode is ExecutionMode.SINGLE_SHOT` identity check (so the `isinstance(execution_params, SingleShotParams)` invariant was not enforced for Nexus-shape commands) and would also have skipped `_validate_maker_preference` for any Nexus-side `MAKER_ONLY` order
- Add `_STP_MODE_VALUE_MAP` (`CANCEL_MAKER` → `EXPIRE_MAKER`, `CANCEL_TAKER` → `EXPIRE_TAKER`, `CANCEL_BOTH` → `EXPIRE_BOTH`) so `translate_stp_mode` carries Nexus's [`nexus.core.stp_mode.STPMode`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/blob/v0.46.0/nexus/core/stp_mode.py) onto the equivalent Praxis [`STPMode`](praxis/core/domain/enums.py) member. Nexus and Praxis are the only enum pair whose value strings disagree (Nexus uses `CANCEL_*`, Praxis uses `EXPIRE_*`) so without the map a generic value-lookup translation would silently throw on every command that carries the Praxis-default `stp_mode`
- Add 16 new tests to [`test_command_translator.py`](tests/test_command_translator.py) covering: each translator passes Praxis members through identity-equal; each translator re-keys a synthetic foreign enum (defined in-file to avoid coupling tests to Nexus internals) to the Praxis member; `STPMode` carries the three `CANCEL_*` → `EXPIRE_*` semantic mappings; and unknown values / non-enum inputs raise `ValueError` / `TypeError` respectively
- Unblocks the deployment-server paper-trade stub-strategy bundle (`StubStrategySFD_4permut`) where the four `always_one` / `always_zero` / `alternating` / `coin_toss` sensors finally produced ENTER actions through the rule-based predict path (post-v0.57.0) but each command was rejected at `validate_trade_command` before it could reach the `BinanceAdapter`. Pre-fix `praxis-1` logs showed `no allowed order types configured for mode SINGLE_SHOT` on every signal tick; post-fix the same UEL routes through `submit_command_with_translated_params` → translated Praxis enums → `validate_trade_command` → `BinanceAdapter.submit_order`. The original `BtcLogRegEVSFD` bundle never tripped this validator because its strategy file's `_reference_price(signal)` already short-circuits on the missing `signal.get('close')` field, so the enum-translation gap was masked end-to-end until the stub bundle started actually firing actions

## v0.58.1 on 10th of May, 2026

- Patch [`translate_maker_preference`](praxis/command_translator.py) and [`translate_stp_mode`](praxis/command_translator.py) in [`praxis/command_translator.py`](praxis/command_translator.py) to substitute `None` with the matching Praxis enum default (`MakerPreference.NO_PREFERENCE` and `STPMode.NONE`, respectively) instead of forwarding `None` to `_translate_enum` and crashing with `TypeError: maker_preference must be MakerPreference or an enum with a string .value, got NoneType`. The strict three (`translate_order_side`, `translate_order_type`, `translate_execution_mode`) keep their `None`-rejecting behaviour because the corresponding Nexus [`Action`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/blob/v0.46.0/nexus/strategy/action.py) fields are guaranteed non-`None` at the ENTER boundary by `Action._validate_action_type_requirements`. Pre-patch `submit_command_with_translated_params` raised `TypeError` on every Nexus `Action` whose strategy did not set `maker_preference` — observed on the deployment server at `2026-05-10T14:31:57Z` for all four `StubStrategySFD_4permut` sensors after the v0.58.0 image landed. Substituting at the boundary keeps the downstream `Trading.submit_command` type contract honest (Praxis enums all the way down, no `Optional` widening of public submit signatures) while preserving the implicit pre-v0.58.0 "no opinion / no STP" semantics
- Update [`tests/test_command_translator.py`](tests/test_command_translator.py): add `test_translate_maker_preference_substitutes_no_preference_for_none`, `test_translate_stp_mode_substitutes_none_member_for_none`, and three `test_translate_*_rejects_none` cases (one per strict translator) pinning the new contracts so a future refactor can re-introduce neither the v0.58.0 deployment-server crash nor an accidental `None` passthrough on a strict field. Foreign-enum re-keying, `STPMode` semantic mapping (`CANCEL_*` → `EXPIRE_*`), and the `TypeError` path for non-`None` non-enum inputs (`translate_order_side('BUY')`) remain unchanged

## v0.59.0 on 11th of May, 2026

- Add `_snap_qty_to_lot_step` helper in [`praxis/infrastructure/binance_adapter.py`](praxis/infrastructure/binance_adapter.py) and call it at the top of `submit_order` so the order quantity is rounded down to the symbol's cached LOT_SIZE step before reaching `_validate_order` and the Binance REST endpoint. Pre-patch the deployment server hit `Order rejected: Illegal characters found in parameter 'quantity'; legal range is '^([0-9]{1,20})(\.[0-9]{1,20})?$'. (code -1100)` on every stub-strategy ENTER tick (observed at `2026-05-10T17:59:03Z` for all four `StubStrategySFD_4permut` sensors after the v0.58.1 image landed): Nexus strategies size ENTER as `notional / reference_price` with no quantization, which produces a `Decimal` carrying the full `getcontext().prec` (default 28) digits — `Decimal('20') / Decimal('81458')` resolves to `0.0002455253013823074467823909254` (31 fractional digits) and `format(qty, 'f')` faithfully renders all of them, overflowing the venue's 20-fractional-digit cap. Snaps via `(qty // lot_step) * lot_step` (floor-divide-then-multiply), not `Decimal.quantize(lot_step, ...)` — `quantize` rounds to the *exponent* of `lot_step` rather than to an integer multiple of it, so `Decimal('0.0002455…').quantize(Decimal('0.00001000'), ROUND_DOWN)` would have produced `0.00024552` (eight fractional digits, neither a multiple of `0.00001` nor accepted by Binance's LOT_SIZE filter). Binance's `exchangeInfo` returns `stepSize='0.00001000'` (exponent `-8`) for BTCUSDT, so the trailing-zero shape is the production case, not a hypothetical. Floor-divide-then-multiply is exact for any `lot_step` shape including non-pure-`10^-n` steps. The snap is a no-op when the symbol's filters are not cached (returns `qty` unchanged), so the existing "No cached filters for X, skipping validation" warning path remains the single signal for a missing filter cache rather than being masked by a silent fallback
- Add `bootstrap_filter_symbols: frozenset[str] = frozenset()` to [`Trading.__init__`](praxis/trading.py) and merge it into the symbol set passed to `VenueAdapter.load_filters` from `_startup_account`. Pre-patch the deployment server logged `No cached filters for BTCUSDT, skipping validation` ahead of every venue submission because [`ExecutionManager.active_symbols`](praxis/core/execution_manager.py) only returns symbols with open orders or positions, which is the empty set on a fresh paper-trade boot — so `load_filters([])` was a no-op, the LOT_SIZE / PRICE / NOTIONAL invariants in `_validate_order` were silently bypassed, and the strategy-supplied high-precision qty reached Binance unsnapped. Wire [`praxis/launcher.py`](praxis/launcher.py) to construct `Trading(...)` with `bootstrap_filter_symbols=frozenset({_DEFAULT_SYMBOL})` so `BTCUSDT` filters are loaded before any sensor can fire its first ENTER action
- Make [`BinanceAdapter.load_filters`](praxis/infrastructure/binance_adapter.py) idempotent: skip symbols already present in `_filters` instead of unconditionally overwriting. `_startup_account` runs `load_filters(union(active_symbols, bootstrap_filter_symbols))` per account, so without the skip every multi-account boot would refetch `exchangeInfo` for the bootstrap symbol once per account — N venue weight units and N round-trips of startup latency for nothing, since per-symbol filter values are deploy-gated and immutable within a process lifetime. In-process filter refresh is intentionally unsupported — the only way to pick up new venue filters is to restart the process; if a future use case ever needs in-process refresh, add an explicit method on the `VenueAdapter` protocol rather than mutating private adapter state from outside
- Add `TestSnapQtyToLotStep` with seven cases in [`tests/test_binance_adapter.py`](tests/test_binance_adapter.py) covering: 31-fractional-digit input snaps to `0.00024` on `lot_step=0.00001`; snap floors (never exceeds input); already-aligned qty preserved unchanged; missing-filter symbol returns the input unchanged (no silent quantize-by-default); `submit_order` end-to-end emits a `quantity=0.00024` URL fragment matching Binance's `^([0-9]{1,20})(\.[0-9]{1,20})?$` regex; the venue-wire-format `lot_step=Decimal('0.00001000')` (with trailing zeros, exponent `-8`) snaps to a value that satisfies BOTH the wire-form-modulo and the numeric-multiple-modulo invariants; and a non-power-of-ten step (`lot_step=5`) floors `13` to `10`. The trailing-zero and non-`10^-n` cases pin the floor-divide-then-multiply contract specifically (a `Decimal.quantize(...)` implementation would silently fail both). Add `test_trading_start_preloads_bootstrap_filter_symbols_on_fresh_boot` and `test_trading_start_merges_bootstrap_and_active_symbols` in [`tests/test_trading.py`](tests/test_trading.py) pinning the new `Trading.__init__` kwarg's behaviour on the fresh-boot and replay-with-orders branches respectively. Existing `test_trading_start_preloads_filters_for_active_symbols` left unchanged — the bootstrap set defaults to empty and the historical behaviour is preserved
- Unblocks the deployment-server paper-trade `StubStrategySFD_4permut` bundle past the venue boundary. With v0.59.0 the four stub sensors' next 900s ENTER ticks are expected to: snap qty to `0.00024` on cached `BTCUSDT` filters → pass `_validate_order` invariants → emit a `quantity=0.00024` REST param that matches Binance's regex → receive a venue ACK (the testnet account has BTCUSDT depth; orders will fill or rest depending on order_type). The four sensors share a `MARKET` order_type via [`logreg_binary_evsfd.py`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/blob/main/manifests/strategies/logreg_binary_evsfd.py) so the expected steady state is immediate fill with the testnet's `lot_step=0.00001` precision. Once an ENTER fills, the strategy's `_exit` path can fire on the next `_preds == 0` tick (for `always_zero`, `alternating`, `coin_toss`) and verify the full ENTER → fill → EXIT → fill → close-position loop end-to-end

## v0.59.1 on 12th of May, 2026

- Anchor [`MarketDataPoller._poll_loop`](praxis/market_data_poller.py)'s fetch cadence to the original schedule (`anchor + n * interval`) instead of resleeping `interval` between fetches, with skip-missed-slots so a multi-interval-overrun fetch does not trigger back-to-back catch-up fetches. Pre-patch the loop did `while not stop_event.wait(timeout=interval): self._fetch(...)` — the wait fired *after* `_fetch` returned, so realized period was `interval + fetch_duration`. On Binance testnet under load `_fetch` regularly takes 200-500s (the `get_spot_klines` HTTP call queues behind rate-limited concurrent requests from the venue adapter), which for `kline_size=300` (5m kline, `interval=300s`) pushed realized period past `_DEFAULT_MAX_AGE_MULTIPLIER * kline_size = 600s` and tripped `StaleMarketDataError` on the next sensor tick — observed on the deployment server at `2026-05-12T07:37:12Z` and recurring every 25-75min thereafter for the `2d9aedcbea82:29` (`logreg_binary_evsfd`) sensor with measured ages of 606-732s. The anchored guarantee is **no cumulative drift**: after each fetch returns, `n` advances to `max(n + 1, int(elapsed // interval) + 1)` so any number of "missed" slots (when a single fetch spans `k > 1` intervals) collapses into a single catch-up fetch at the next future scheduled slot rather than firing `k - 1` back-to-back fetches with `wait(0)`. The freshness goal of skipped slots is moot — the slow fetch's return brings data through `now` — and the post-overrun cadence resumes immediately at the original timeline. `n` slow fetches therefore do not produce `n * overrun` cumulative drift
- Apply the same skip-missed-slots logic to `n`'s initialization after the *initial* fetch: `n = max(1, int(elapsed // interval) + 1)`. Pre-patch this commit hardcoded `n = 1` after the explicit pre-loop fetch, so a slow initial fetch (e.g. cold-cache exchangeInfo round-trip + first historical kline fetch) caused iter 1's `wait_seconds = max(0, 1 * interval - elapsed)` to fall to 0 and fire fetch #2 immediately back-to-back — exactly the burst skip-missed-slots is meant to prevent. Now the initial fetch's overrun follows the same skip rule as every subsequent fetch
- Extract the n-advancement math into a module-level [`_next_slot_index(anchor, interval, now, min_n)`](praxis/market_data_poller.py) helper with an FP-safety while-loop bump. The naive `int((now - anchor) // interval) + 1` formula is *almost always* correct, but binary FP rounding can leave `anchor + n * interval == now` exactly for unlucky pairs — e.g. `elapsed=1.0, interval=0.1` gives `n=10` and `10 * 0.1 == 1.0` exactly, so `wait_seconds = max(0, anchor + n * interval - now) = 0` would fire the next fetch back-to-back, defeating skip-missed-slots. The helper's `while anchor + n * interval <= now: n += 1` enforces the strict-future invariant; runs 0 times in the common case, 1-2 times in the FP-corner case. Both n-advancement sites in `_poll_loop` (after the initial pre-loop fetch and after each in-loop fetch) call the helper now, also collapsing the previous "two `time.monotonic()` reads per advance step" into a single `now`. Add four `TestNextSlotIndex` cases pinning the strict-future invariant for the exact-multiple FP corner case (`elapsed=1.0, interval=0.1`), the FP-undercount case (`0.3 // 0.1 == 2.0`), the `min_n` floor enforcement, and the `min_n=1` initial-fetch contract — verified that removing the while-loop bump deterministically fails the exact-multiple test
- Widen the `interval` type hint from `int` to `float` on [`_PollerThread.interval`](praxis/market_data_poller.py), [`MarketDataPoller.add_kline_size`](praxis/market_data_poller.py), `MarketDataPoller.__init__`'s `kline_intervals: dict[int, float]`, and the internal `_start_thread_locked` / `_poll_loop` signatures. Production callers continue to pass integer seconds (`300`, `900`); the regression tests need sub-second cadences (`0.1`, `0.105`) to actually run in CI time. `kline_size` stays `int` (Binance kline sizes are all integer seconds). Tighten the existing positivity check from `interval <= 0` to `not math.isfinite(interval) or interval <= 0` so the float widening cannot smuggle in `NaN` (slips through `<= 0` because any comparison with NaN is False, then propagates to `wait_seconds = anchor + n * NaN - now = NaN` and breaks `Event.wait`) or `inf` (passes `<= 0`, makes `wait(inf)` block the poller thread forever with no re-fetches). Add `test_add_kline_size_rejects_nan_and_inf_interval` and `test_start_rejects_nan_and_inf_initial_intervals` pinning the new invariant on both validation paths
- Add three regression tests in [`tests/test_market_data_poller.py`](tests/test_market_data_poller.py): `test_slow_fetch_does_not_accumulate_drift_in_fetch_schedule` pins cumulative-no-drift for sub-2-interval overruns (anchored fetch #4 at `4 * interval = 0.40s` vs pre-fix `3 * interval + slow_delay = 0.45s`, threshold `0.425s`); `test_multi_interval_slow_fetch_collapses_missed_slots` pins the in-loop skip behaviour for `slow_delay = 2.5 * interval` (fetch #3 lands at next future slot, not back-to-back with slow return); `test_slow_initial_fetch_skips_to_next_future_slot` pins the initial-fetch skip for `slow_delay = 1.05 * interval` (fetch #2 at next slot `0.20s` vs pre-fix back-to-back at `0.105s`, threshold `0.1525s` with ~`0.045s` margin each side). All three regressions verified locally by swapping the loop body to each pre-state implementation and confirming the corresponding test fails before restoring

## v0.59.2 on 13th of May, 2026

- Fix [`EventSpine`](praxis/infrastructure/event_spine.py) silent-data-loss-across-restart bug: `append()` and `ensure_schema()` now call `await self._conn.commit()` after every successful write, so events become durable on the main DB file before the method returns. Pre-patch the spine relied on `aiosqlite`'s default `isolation_level=""` (implicit transactions) and never committed — every insert sat in the rollback journal, invisible to any other connection, and rolled back on connection close without explicit `commit()` (and on crash/unclean shutdown). Observed in production at `2026-05-13T03:50Z`: the host-mounted `/opt/praxis/state/event_spine.sqlite` showed main DB modtime stuck on `2026-05-05` (the boot date when the schema was first created via `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`) while a 25KB rollback journal file was being actively written; a separate `sqlite3.connect(...)` to the same path returned `events count = 0` despite the running launcher having written 83+ trade-flow events that were visible only to its own connection. Each `docker compose up -d` recreate (e.g. for an image bump) wiped the entire prior session's spine. Per-event `commit()` is the minimal correct fix: matches the at-least-once log semantics callers already assume (`await append(...)` returning a `seq` means "this event is on disk"), and per-event `fsync` cost is negligible against the venue REST round trip on the same critical path
- Add `test_append_commits_so_separate_connection_sees_event` and `test_append_survives_writer_connection_loss` in [`tests/test_event_spine.py`](tests/test_event_spine.py). Both tests use a real file-backed sqlite (not `:memory:`) so the cross-connection visibility property is actually exercised. The first opens a separate `sqlite3.connect()` after `await spine.append(...)` and asserts the row count = 1; the second writes 5 events, explicitly `await writer_conn.close()` with no extra `commit()` (matches the production failure mode where `aiosqlite.Connection.close()` forwards to `sqlite3.Connection.close()` which does not commit pending transactions), opens a fresh connection on the same file, and asserts all 5 events are still on disk. Verified locally that both tests fail deterministically when the new `commit()` calls are removed

## v0.60.0 on 15th of May, 2026

- Add [`praxis/market_data_cache.py`](praxis/market_data_cache.py) with `MainCache` (disk-persisted 1-min kline cache fed daily from Limen's HF dataset and per-minute from `binancial.get_spot_klines`, both writing to one parquet under a single `_write_lock`) and `CacheScheduler` (two daemon threads driving the two refresh paths). Unit tests in [`tests/test_market_data_cache.py`](tests/test_market_data_cache.py) cover refresh paths, source-aware overlap precedence, atomic writes, concurrent refresh, scheduler lifecycle + restart-after-dead-thread, snapshot, corrupt-state / corrupt-parquet / future-state self-heal, and finite-value validation for scheduler intervals ([Vaquum/Praxis#108](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/108))
- Gut [`praxis/market_data_poller.py`](praxis/market_data_poller.py) from a per-kline_size poller-thread pool (502 LOC) to a thin staleness-aware adapter over `MainCache` (~215 LOC). `MarketDataPoller(cache, max_age_overrides)`; `get_market_data` and `is_stale` semantics + `StaleMarketDataError` shape preserved for existing callers; lifecycle is owned by `CacheScheduler`

- Fix [`praxis/market_data_cache.py`](praxis/market_data_cache.py) `_apply_new_bars` to normalize every column dtype in `new_bars` to match the in-memory `_frame`'s schema before concat. The Limen HF parquet stores `Datetime('ms', 'UTC')` and `UInt64` on `no_of_trades`, while binancial's pandas → polars frame produces `Datetime('us', 'UTC')` and `Int64`. Pre-fix `pl.concat` raised `polars.exceptions.SchemaError` on the first mismatched column and the cache froze at Limen's high-water mark. Production-observed after the v0.60.0 first-boot Limen bootstrap on 2026-05-16; the scheduler logged the same SchemaError every 60s, the boot binancial refresh failed too, and the cache stayed at `2026-05-15 23:59:00 UTC` until the fix landed

## v0.60.2 on 17th of May, 2026

- Fix [`praxis/infrastructure/event_spine.py`](praxis/infrastructure/event_spine.py) `append(FillReceived)` to use Python sqlite3's implicit BEGIN-on-DML + explicit `commit()` / `rollback()` instead of `SAVEPOINT fill_atomic` / `RELEASE` / `ROLLBACK TO`. Pre-fix the SAVEPOINT path interacted poorly with the default `isolation_level=""` semantics: non-DML statements (`SAVEPOINT`, `RELEASE`, `ROLLBACK TO`) trigger an implicit `COMMIT` before they run, which collapses the savepoint stack. In production this surfaced as `sqlite3.OperationalError: no such savepoint: fill_atomic` on every `RELEASE` and produced 23 errors over 22h of paper trading (19 `on_message callback error` from the WebSocket fill stream + 4 `unhandled exception while processing command` from the command-dispatch loop, both call sites hitting the same broken `event_spine.append`). The new pattern wraps the dedup-insert + event-insert pair in Python's auto-begun transaction and uses explicit `commit()` on success / `rollback()` on failure — atomicity preserved, no SAVEPOINT pitfalls
- Add [`tests/test_event_spine.py`](tests/test_event_spine.py) `test_fill_atomicity_many_consecutive_appends_no_savepoint_error` — exercises 50 consecutive `FillReceived` appends (the same shape that produced the production errors) and asserts no `OperationalError` is raised
- Add `curl` to [`Dockerfile`](Dockerfile) and a Docker `HEALTHCHECK` directive that probes `http://localhost:${PORT:-${HEALTHZ_PORT:-8080}}/healthz` every 30s after a 10-minute `start-period` (covers the first-ever boot's synchronous Limen HF snapshot download per TD-061). Pre-fix the image shipped no probe tool and no `HEALTHCHECK` was declared, so `docker exec ... curl /healthz` failed (binary missing) and `docker ps` showed no `Health` column. Post-fix `docker ps` reports `(healthy)` / `(unhealthy)` and a hung launcher surfaces within ~90s of the first failed probe
- Apply round-1 review feedback on the SAVEPOINT fix: (a) rewrote `append()`'s docstring to drop the stale SAVEPOINT mention (the implementation comment block in the function body retains the historical context); (b) split the FillReceived try/except into separate DML and commit blocks so a commit failure now propagates the COMMIT exception, not a rollback failure that masks it; new `_safe_rollback()` helper does best-effort rollback that logs but does not raise; (c) added file-backed `spine_file` fixture (uses `aiosqlite.connect(tmp_path/.sqlite)`) for the regression test so it reproduces the same transaction semantics as production (the default `:memory:` fixture has subtly different journal/transaction behavior and did NOT reproduce the original SAVEPOINT bug); pinned the new commit-failure and rollback-failure paths with `test_fill_atomicity_commit_failure_preserves_original_exception` and `test_fill_atomicity_rollback_failure_does_not_mask_dml_exception`
- Bump `vaquum-nexus` pin to v0.48.0 (`Nexus@836ad76`) to pick up Nexus PR #68 — INFO-level `signal produced` log emitted at every sensor tick before strategy dispatch, with full type-safe coercion of `Signal.values` for the orjson renderer
- Apply round-3 review feedback: (a) parameterize the [`Dockerfile`](Dockerfile) `HEALTHCHECK` port via `${PORT:-${HEALTHZ_PORT:-8080}}` so the probe stays in sync with whichever env var the operator / platform actually sets (matches the launcher's resolution order in [`praxis/launcher.py`](praxis/launcher.py)); (b) drop the stale `wget` reference in this v0.60.2 entry (the fix added `curl`, not `wget`); (c) switch the three `test_fill_atomicity_*` direct-attribute-restore patterns in [`tests/test_event_spine.py`](tests/test_event_spine.py) to pytest's `monkeypatch` fixture so a mid-test assertion failure cannot leave the connection's `commit`/`rollback`/`_append_event` replaced with a failing stub (restore now happens in pytest teardown regardless of test outcome)

## v0.61.0 on 19th of May, 2026

- Add `praxis.binsim` package — an in-process Binance simulator that replaces Binance testnet as Praxis's paper-trading venue, addresses issue #112. Removes testnet-borne incidents (recvWindow rejections from clock skew, per-IP weight pinning `BinanceAdapter._used_weight`, rate-throttling) and unlocks the same backend for high-speed replay later
- Add [`praxis/binsim/book.py`](praxis/binsim/book.py) `OrderBook` — single-symbol top-N snapshot with `replace(bids, asks, last_update_id, ts_ms)` (validates non-empty, positive prices/qtys, strict descending bids / ascending asks, no crossed book, monotonic update id) and non-mutating `consume_qty_for_market_order(side, qty)` that walks the book and returns the fill ladder; returns partial fills when the book is exhausted, raises on empty book / non-positive qty
- Add [`praxis/binsim/feed.py`](praxis/binsim/feed.py) `DepthPoller` — `aiohttp.ClientSession` poll loop against `https://binance-spot-depth20-1000ms.onrender.com/top20` at 1000ms cadence, Bearer auth from env, exposes `last_success_ts_ms` so the HTTP layer's staleness gate has a freshness read. Split exception handling logs transient network errors at WARNING (`aiohttp.ClientError`/`TimeoutError`) and malformed-payload errors at ERROR (`ValueError`/`KeyError`) so a persistently broken upstream is louder than a flaky network
- Add [`praxis/binsim/ledger.py`](praxis/binsim/ledger.py) `Ledger` — per-account `{USDT, BTC, fills, seen_client_order_ids}` state under a single `asyncio.Lock`; atomic snapshot via `tempfile.mkstemp` + `Path.replace` (same pattern as `MainCache._atomic_write_main_cache_state`) after every mutation. Two settlement APIs: `apply_fill(...)` (single fill, no dedup, used by tests) and `apply_order(account_id, side, fills, client_order_id)` (atomic multi-level with `client_order_id` dedup, used by the HTTP POST handler). Returns assigned monotonic `order_id` + per-fill monotonic `trade_id`s. Fee model is USDT-only for MMVP — `apply_fill` raises on `fee_asset != 'USDT'`
- Add [`praxis/binsim/server.py`](praxis/binsim/server.py) `BinsimServer` + `make_app` — `aiohttp.web.Application` wiring all binsim routes plus a lifecycle wrapper that owns `AppRunner` + `TCPSite`. Deps injected via typed `web.AppKey` instances (`BOOK_KEY`, `LEDGER_KEY`, `POLLER_KEY`, `STALENESS_THRESHOLD_MS_KEY`, `WS_SUBSCRIPTION_COUNTER_KEY`)
- Add binsim HTTP routes: `GET /healthz` (`{"status":"ok"}`), `GET /api/v3/time` (`{serverTime: <unix-ms>}`), `GET /api/v3/exchangeInfo` (static BTCUSDT filter response — `PRICE_FILTER tickSize=0.01`, `LOT_SIZE step/min=1e-5 max=9000`, `NOTIONAL min=5`), `GET /api/v3/depth` (book snapshot in Binance shape with symbol+limit validation), `GET /api/v3/account` (ledger balances), and signed stubs `GET /api/v3/order` (404 + Binance code -2013), `GET /api/v3/openOrders` (`[]`), `GET /api/v3/myTrades` (`[]`)
- Add binsim `POST /api/v3/order` — HMAC presence check → staleness gate (`now_ms - last_success_ts_ms > threshold_ms` → 503 + Binance code -1003) → param validation (symbol/side/type/quantity/newClientOrderId + Binance-shaped 400 codes -1100 / -1121) → `OrderBook.consume_qty_for_market_order` → liquidity check (sum-of-walked-qty < requested → 400 + -2010) → per-fill 10bps taker commission → atomic `Ledger.apply_order` (insufficient balance / duplicate clientOrderId → 400 + -2010) → Binance FULL response shape with `fills[].{tradeId, price, qty, commission, commissionAsset}` matching Praxis's `_parse_submit_response`
- Add HMAC presence helper `_require_signed_caller` — checks `X-MBX-APIKEY` header + `signature` query param + resolves `api_key` → `account_id` via `Ledger.account_for_api_key` (which hashes the incoming key and looks it up in the index built from registered accounts). Per-MMVP spec, the binsim does presence checking only, not full HMAC verify
- Add binsim WebSocket routes: `/ws-api/v3` (accepts `userDataStream.subscribe.signature`, hands out monotonic `subscriptionId` via per-app `_WsSubscriptionCounter`, validates api_key via `Ledger.account_for_api_key`, error frames mirror Binance `{id, status, error: {code, msg}}` shape — 401/-1022 for bad sig, 401/-2014 for unknown key, 400/-1100 for unknown method) and `/stream` (accepts connection but pushes no frames; aiohttp built-in `heartbeat=180s` keeps the connection alive past idle timeout)
- Add [`praxis/binsim/__main__.py`](praxis/binsim/__main__.py) CLI entrypoint — reads env (`BINSIM_DEPTH_TOKEN`, `BINSIM_STATE_DIR` required; `BINSIM_HOST`, `BINSIM_PORT`, `BINSIM_DEPTH_URL`, `BINSIM_STALENESS_MS`, `BINSIM_POLL_INTERVAL_MS` optional), wires `OrderBook` + `Ledger.load()` + `DepthPoller.start()` + `BinsimServer.start()`, registers SIGTERM/SIGINT handlers BEFORE starting long-lived components for deterministic shutdown. The poller's background loop primes the book on its first iteration; the staleness gate trips until it succeeds
- Add `BINSIM_URL` paper-mode override in [`praxis/launcher.py`](praxis/launcher.py) `_resolve_trade_mode` — when set under `TRADE_MODE=paper`, derives REST/WS/WS-API URLs from a single `http(s)://host:port` base (`http`→`ws`, `https`→`wss`, WS-API path hard-coded to `/ws-api/v3`). Mixing `BINSIM_URL` with `TRADE_MODE=live` is a hard `RuntimeError`. Bad scheme (not http/https) or missing host raises with a clear message
- Relax the `wss://` scheme check in [`praxis/infrastructure/binance_ws.py`](praxis/infrastructure/binance_ws.py) `BinanceUserStream.initiate_connection` to accept `ws://` only when the `BINSIM_URL` env var is set. Production deployments without `BINSIM_URL` retain the original fail-closed wss-only behavior — the relaxation is gated so a mis-configured deployment cannot fall back to an insecure scheme by accident. Docstrings on `_setup_connection` and `_clean_setup_connection` updated to mention the gated `ws://` allowance
- Add [`Dockerfile.binsim`](Dockerfile.binsim) — `python:3.12-slim` base, installs the same wheel as the main Praxis image but uses a separate `binsim:1000` user, `/var/lib/binsim` state dir, `BINSIM_STATE_DIR=/var/lib/binsim` + `BINSIM_PORT=8081` baked-in defaults, `HEALTHCHECK` probes `${BINSIM_PORT}/healthz` with `start-period=30s` (no Limen download needed, fast boot), `ENTRYPOINT ["python", "-m", "praxis.binsim"]`
- Add [`.github/workflows/build_image_binsim.yml`](.github/workflows/build_image_binsim.yml) — parallel to `build_image.yml`, pushes to a separate `ghcr.io/vaquum/praxis-binsim` repo with scoped buildx cache so it does not contend with the main image cache. Same tag scheme (`sha-<sha>`, `<version>`, `main`)
- Add 257 binsim tests across [`tests/test_binsim_book.py`](tests/test_binsim_book.py) (34, OrderBook validation + walk), [`tests/test_binsim_feed.py`](tests/test_binsim_feed.py) (23, poller lifecycle + auth header + failure modes), [`tests/test_binsim_ledger.py`](tests/test_binsim_ledger.py) (55, balance math + atomic snapshot + concurrent fills + dedup), [`tests/test_binsim_server.py`](tests/test_binsim_server.py) (51, every HTTP endpoint), [`tests/test_binsim_ws.py`](tests/test_binsim_ws.py) (9, WS handshake), [`tests/test_binsim_main.py`](tests/test_binsim_main.py) (20, env parsing), [`tests/test_binsim_binance_adapter_smoke.py`](tests/test_binsim_binance_adapter_smoke.py) (9, end-to-end against the real `BinanceAdapter`), and [`tests/test_binsim_execution_manager_smoke.py`](tests/test_binsim_execution_manager_smoke.py) (5, end-to-end through `ExecutionManager` asserting the `CommandAccepted → OrderSubmitIntent → OrderSubmitted → FillReceived` event-spine sequence)
- Bump 8 launcher trade-mode tests in [`tests/test_launcher_trade_mode.py`](tests/test_launcher_trade_mode.py) covering `BINSIM_URL` derivation, https→wss, empty-fallback-to-testnet, live-mode hard-block, scheme/host validation. Add 2 [`tests/test_binance_ws.py`](tests/test_binance_ws.py) tests for the `ws://` scheme gating (rejected without `BINSIM_URL`, accepted with it)
- Note TD-064, TD-065, TD-066 in [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md) for the MMVP-deferred items surfaced during pre-PR review: `LOT_SIZE` filter not enforced in `POST /api/v3/order`, hardcoded process-wide taker fee rate vs the issue spec's per-account fee model, and unbounded `seen_client_order_ids` set growth
- Refactor binsim api_key management: drop the operator-supplied `BINSIM_API_KEYS` env var and the `api_keys` parameter from `make_app` / `BinsimServer`. `Ledger.register_account` now mints a fresh 64-hex-char api_key via `secrets.token_hex(32)`, persists it as part of the account snapshot, and returns it. The HTTP layer's signed handlers resolve `X-MBX-APIKEY` via `Ledger.account_for_api_key`. Adds a `register` subcommand to the CLI: `python -m praxis.binsim register --account-id <id> --initial-usdt <amount> [--initial-btc <amount>]` mints the key and prints it on stdout for the operator to paste into Praxis's `BINANCE_API_KEY_<acct>` env. Removes a layer of operator config drift — one place to register accounts, one place the api_key exists

## v0.63.0 on 21st of May, 2026

- Bump `vaquum-nexus` pin from `836ad76` (v0.48.0) to `a37bff5` (v0.49.0) in [`pyproject.toml`](pyproject.toml). The new Nexus revision adds `_inject_reference_price` in [`nexus/strategy/signal_producer.py`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/blob/a37bff5e657cc88d08b25e04b85770acc0d0f945/nexus/strategy/signal_producer.py), which forwards the latest raw `close` from the pre-feature-engineering `market_data` frame into `Signal.values` when the predictor doesn't include `close` itself. Unblocks the deployed `logreg_binary_evsfd` sensor (and any future ML sensor whose `predict()` runs `close` through `fractional_diff` or any other column-replacing transform) from the silent-no-op failure mode where every `_preds=1` produced zero commands because the strategy's `_reference_price(signal)` returned `None`. Stub-pattern predictors that forward `close` directly are unaffected — the shim never overwrites a `close` the predictor already supplied

## v0.64.0 on 23rd of May, 2026

### Update

- Bump `vaquum_limen` git+https pin from `v3.0.6` to `v3.9.0` ([Vaquum/Limen@v3.9.0](https://github.com/Vaquum/Limen/releases/tag/v3.9.0)) in [`pyproject.toml`](pyproject.toml). Limen v3.9.0 ships the new YAML-based SFD pipeline (`limen/yaml/` package — `parser`, `compiler`, `resolver`, `validator`, `rules`) that unblocks experiment_runner bundles declaring `metadata.json["sfd_module"] = "yaml:<name>"` with a `manifest.yml` instead of a `<sfd_module>.py` predictor file. Without this bump the existing `vaquum_limen 3.0.6` `Trainer.__init__` raises `ValueError` on the `yaml:` prefix (`:` is not a valid Python identifier segment per the path-traversal hardening at [`trainer.py:158`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Limen/blob/v3.9.0/limen/experiment/trainer/trainer.py#L158)) before any bundle file is read, so the new bundle shape cannot wire at startup
- Re-verify Praxis's direct Limen imports against v3.9.0. [`praxis/market_data_cache.py`](praxis/market_data_cache.py) imports `HistoricalData` (public) and the private `_aggregate_spot_klines as _limen_aggregate_spot_klines` (lines 33-35; the private dependency is acknowledged in the docstring at lines 465-466 with a TD note in [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md) about pushing Limen for a public API). Both still resolve in v3.9.0 with the same signatures — `HistoricalData.get_spot_klines(row_count_limit=None, kline_size=60, start_date_limit=None, end_date_limit=None, *, n_rows=None) -> pl.DataFrame` (line 762 of Limen's v3.9.0 historical_data.py uses it), `_aggregate_spot_klines(data: pl.DataFrame, kline_size: int) -> pl.DataFrame` (defined at line 408). The re-validate-on-upgrade contract that motivated the TD note holds for this jump. Mirrors the Nexus v0.45.0 / v0.50.0 precedent of explicitly documenting private-attribute / private-function re-validation on every Limen pin bump
- Bump `vaquum-nexus` pin from `a37bff5` (v0.49.0) to `6d6af60` (v0.50.0) in [`pyproject.toml`](pyproject.toml). The new Nexus revision lands the matching `vaquum_limen v3.0.6 → v3.9.0` pin bump ([Vaquum/Nexus#70](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/pull/70)) so the two transitively-pinned `vaquum_limen` versions agree (Praxis's pip resolver refuses to install otherwise). Nexus also refreshes the two `# NOTE … as of vaquum_limen 3.0.6` comments in [`nexus/startup/sequencer.py`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/blob/6d6af6056567e1571ad2353ec660b89283474e50/nexus/startup/sequencer.py) (lines 631, 651) to `3.9.0` so the private-attribute re-validate-on-upgrade marker reflects the version Nexus 0.50.0 actually validates against. No Nexus production code changed by the Limen bump; the public `Trainer` / `RuleBasedManifest` surfaces consumed by `produce_signal` / `_wire_sensors` are stable across the v3.0.6 → v3.9.0 jump (verified by Nexus's 10 `test_limen_trainer_contract.py` cases plus the rule-based / ML-manifest branch tests in `test_signal_producer.py` — all 1385 Nexus tests pass against v3.9.0 unmodified)
- Add transitive runtime dependency on `ruamel.yaml>=0.18` via Limen v3.9.0 (`limen/yaml/parser.py` imports `from ruamel.yaml import YAML`). Picked up automatically through Limen's `pyproject.toml` dependency list — Praxis does not import `ruamel.yaml` directly, but loading any `yaml:`-prefixed SFD bundle through Nexus's `Trainer` will trigger the import chain. Praxis's full pytest suite passes against the new stack (1352 passed, 1 skipped, 3 pre-existing `binance` / `websockets` deprecation warnings — unrelated to the Limen / Nexus bump)
- Note TD-067 in [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md): no Praxis-side integration test exists for the YAML-backed SFD bundle launch path enabled by this bump. The existing [`tests/test_launcher_sfd_path.py`](tests/test_launcher_sfd_path.py) only covers the legacy Python-module SFD path (`importlib.import_module(metadata['sfd_module'])`); the YAML path bypasses that helper entirely. Meaningful regression coverage lives upstream in Limen's [`tests/test_yaml.py`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Limen/blob/v3.9.0/tests/test_yaml.py) (975 lines) and Nexus's [`tests/test_limen_trainer_contract.py`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/blob/6d6af6056567e1571ad2353ec660b89283474e50/tests/test_limen_trainer_contract.py) (10 cases). When to fix: once the experiment_runner bundle format stabilizes against a tagged Limen version (currently the downstream artifact `btc_logreg_15m_up_early__r0024` ships with `limen_version: "3.9.2"` — unreleased dev), or when the next YAML-bundle deploy lands and a real production bundle can serve as the fixture template

## v0.66.0 on 25th of May, 2026

### NOTE

- Epoch-scoping `state_dir` / `strategy_state_path` (see Fix below) changes the on-disk layout: on first boot of v0.66.0, state written by ≤v0.65.0 under the old account-level paths (`STATE_BASE / <account_id>`, `STRATEGY_STATE_BASE / <account_id>`) is not recovered — even with an unchanged `EPOCH_ID`. The account starts from a fresh `InstanceState` (`capital_pool` re-read from the manifest, positions rebuilt from the venue at boot). This one-time reset is intended; old directories are left in place (TD-068). To preserve continuity, copy the old account-level tree (`STATE_BASE / <account_id>/{snapshots,wal,strategy_state}`, plus `STRATEGY_STATE_BASE / <account_id>/` if set) into the matching `<EPOCH_ID>/` subdirectories before upgrading — see [`docs/Launcher.md`](docs/Launcher.md)

### Fix

- Fold `EPOCH_ID` into the per-account InstanceState path in [`praxis/launcher.py`](praxis/launcher.py) `main()` — `state_dir` and `strategy_state_path` are now `STATE_BASE / <account_id> / <epoch_id>` and `STRATEGY_STATE_BASE / <account_id> / <epoch_id>` rather than `… / <account_id>`. Previously the snapshot/WAL dir was account-scoped only, so an `EPOCH_ID` bump reset the event spine (epoch-scoped by column) but **not** the InstanceState: `StateStore.recover()` found the prior epoch's snapshot + WAL and restored the persisted `capital_pool` instead of taking the fresh `InstanceState.fresh(manifest.capital_pool)` path. A manifest `capital_pool` change across an epoch was therefore silently ignored — orders were sized off the new manifest pool but budget-checked against the stale recovered pool (`compute_strategy_budget` reads `state.capital_pool`), so every ENTER was denied at the CAPITAL stage (`CAPITAL_RESERVATION_DENIED`) and the deployment produced signals but zero trades. Folding the epoch into the path makes an epoch bump yield a fresh InstanceState (`recover()` → `None` → fresh from manifest), matching the event spine's existing epoch isolation; crash recovery within an epoch is unchanged (same epoch → same dir → same snapshot/WAL). Closes #120
- Update the `_require_safe_account_id` docstring in [`praxis/launcher.py`](praxis/launcher.py) to reflect the `STATE_BASE / <account_id> / <epoch_id>` layout
- Update operator-facing [`docs/Launcher.md`](docs/Launcher.md) (`STATE_BASE` / `STRATEGY_STATE_BASE` rows) for the epoch-scoped path layout, and note that strategy state falls back under `state_dir` when `STRATEGY_STATE_BASE` is unset
- Add [`tests/test_launcher_state_dir_epoch.py`](tests/test_launcher_state_dir_epoch.py) — asserts `main()` builds both `state_dir` and `strategy_state_path` with the `EPOCH_ID` path component
- Note TD-068 in [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md): old per-account / superseded-epoch state dirs (under both `STATE_BASE` and `STRATEGY_STATE_BASE`) are never reaped

## v0.67.0 on 26th of May, 2026

- Bump `vaquum-nexus` pin from `952f6fc` (v0.51.0) to `9d249d7` (v0.52.0) in [`pyproject.toml`](pyproject.toml). v0.52.0 reworks [`_wire_sensors`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/blob/9d249d7faa89aba3738982aed86d31a3ae60efdf/nexus/startup/sequencer.py) with two opt-in, env-gated accelerators for Limen `Sensor` reconstruction, both defaulting to the prior serial behavior so an unconfigured deployment wires exactly as before: process-parallel reconstruction across `NEXUS_WIRE_MAX_WORKERS` worker processes (default `1` = inline, since `train()` is GIL-bound and gains nothing from threads) and a reconstruct-once disk cache under `NEXUS_SENSOR_CACHE_DIR` (unset = off), keyed by a SHA-256 over the bundle's `metadata.json` + `manifest.yml` and the `permutation_id`, that auto-invalidates on `limen_version` / data-window change. Effect for this deployment: the ~5000-permutation `btc_logreg` bundle that previously took ~8h to wire serially on every boot — and re-wired in full on each restart / `EPOCH_ID` bump — can now fan reconstruction across cores and load already-reconstructed sensors from disk, making restarts and epoch bumps near-instant once the cache is warm. No Praxis code change is required; the launcher opts in purely by setting the two environment variables in the deployment environment

## v0.68.0 on 28th of May, 2026

### NOTE

- Operational migration: Nexus v0.52.1 (transited by this bump) is a **BREAKING** change for any deployment that set `NEXUS_WIRE_MAX_WORKERS > 1` in v0.67.0. As of v0.52.1, `_wire_sensors` no longer runs a `ProcessPoolExecutor` in the launcher; `NEXUS_WIRE_MAX_WORKERS` now governs the standalone pre-launch warmer ([`python -m nexus.startup.warm_cache`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/blob/bb2356b0ebeaa0069e5c6a07a339edf34063283c/nexus/startup/warm_cache.py)) instead of in-launcher wiring. The warmer reconstructs every sensor in a process that never imports Polars, writes the disk cache, and exits before the launcher starts; the launcher then wires inline against the warm cache. The root cause was a Polars rayon worker-stack segfault — creating a worker pool in the launcher (which owns the global rayon pool for the market-data cache) degraded that pool, and a later large Polars merge segfaulted the process every ~37 min on the 5000-permutation deploy. A deployment previously parallelising sensor wiring via `NEXUS_WIRE_MAX_WORKERS > 1` must add the warmer invocation to its pre-launch step; an unconfigured deployment (warmer absent, `NEXUS_WIRE_MAX_WORKERS` unset) wires inline and is unchanged. Closes the segfault crash-loop observed on prod

### Update

- Bump `vaquum-nexus` pin from `9d249d7` (v0.52.0) to `bb2356b` ([Nexus PR #74](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/pull/74), v0.52.1 + v0.53.0) in [`pyproject.toml`](pyproject.toml). v0.53.0 replaces [`PredictLoop`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/blob/bb2356b0ebeaa0069e5c6a07a339edf34063283c/nexus/strategy/predict_loop.py)'s bounded `ThreadPoolExecutor` with a `spawn` `ProcessPoolExecutor` so the GIL-bound Limen `MLManifest.prepare_data` (~4.4 s/predict, ~99.9% Python/Polars on a ~224k-row frame) parallelises across cores. Measured on the prod-equivalent 5000-permutation 15m deploy: 16 spawn workers with `POLARS_MAX_THREADS=1` per worker = 6.37 predicts/sec (required 5.05/sec to clear the 15-minute cadence SLA); threads previously yielded ~0.3/sec, missing SLA by ~24x. Parent-side strategy dispatch, context lookup, and action submission are serialised by the pool's result-handling thread, so capital / WAL / risk plumbing is untouched. Per-cycle Arrow IPC sharing of the aggregated market-data frame (one parent aggregation per `(kline_size, latest_bar)` written to a refcounted temp IPC file) means 5000 sensors sharing one kline pay one parent aggregation per cycle, not one per sensor. New env knobs: `NEXUS_PREDICT_MAX_WORKERS` (default `16`) and `NEXUS_PREDICT_POLARS_MAX_THREADS` (default `1`, set via `os.environ.setdefault('POLARS_MAX_THREADS', ...)` before pool creation so spawned worker interpreters inherit it). No Praxis code change is required; the launcher passes the same `market_data_provider` / `context_provider` / `action_submit` callables it always did. v0.52.1 (transited) ships the pre-launch warmer described in the NOTE above
- Pin transits two minor Nexus releases (v0.52.1, v0.53.0); commit body for the bump references both and the operational migration note. Praxis's full pytest suite passes against the new stack unmodified

## v0.68.1 on 29th of May, 2026

### NOTE

- Hotfix bump for two prod-surfaced regressions introduced by v0.68.0's transit of Nexus v0.53.0. The v0.53.0 `ThreadPoolExecutor → spawn ProcessPoolExecutor` swap in [`PredictLoop`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/blob/2cae5c044d427954a82b7628cc54a16229ea56bf/nexus/strategy/predict_loop.py) crossed a process boundary for the first time, surfacing a latent `Signal` pickling defect; concurrent dispatch onto `state.positions` while [`serialize_state`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/blob/2cae5c044d427954a82b7628cc54a16229ea56bf/nexus/infrastructure/wal_codec.py) iterated the live dict surfaced a `RuntimeError: dictionary changed size during iteration` that demoted the operational mode to `REDUCE_ONLY` for ~26h until the next epoch reset. Both fixes were monkeypatch-validated on prod as `praxis:0.68.0-mp1` (run 14:17–15:58 UTC on 2026-05-28: 16,241 `signal produced`, 4,253 `command submitted`, 0 pickling errors, 0 restarts) before being promoted to a tagged Nexus release. No Praxis code change is required; the pin bump alone delivers the upstream fixes

### Update

- Bump `vaquum-nexus` pin from `bb2356b` (v0.53.0) to `2cae5c0` ([Nexus PR #75](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/pull/75), v0.53.1) in [`pyproject.toml`](pyproject.toml). v0.53.1 ships two fixes: (1) `Signal.__reduce__` so a `Signal` returned from a spawn `ProcessPoolExecutor` worker survives the pool's result-pickle path — `Signal.__post_init__` wraps `values` in `types.MappingProxyType` for immutability, which is not picklable by default, so the v0.53.0 spawn pool raised `TypeError: cannot pickle 'mappingproxy' object` inside `_sendback_result` on the first predict cycle of every deploy; `__reduce__` ships `values` as a plain `dict` across the wire, the receiving side re-runs `__post_init__` and re-wraps it, and consumers still see `MappingProxyType`. (2) `serialize_state` now snapshots every mutable dict it iterates via `dict()` before the per-item encode comprehensions — covers `state.positions`, `state.strategy_modes`, `state.capital.per_strategy_deployed`, and `state.risk.per_strategy`; `wal_lock` is innermost in the lock chain so `serialize_state` runs without `positions_lock` / `CapitalController._lock` and cannot exclude concurrent writers. No Praxis code change is required; the parent-side dispatch path is unchanged
- Praxis's full pytest suite passes against the new stack unmodified

## v0.68.2 on 30th of May, 2026

### NOTE

- `_resolve_trade_mode` previously forced `market_data_testnet=True` under `TRADE_MODE=paper` regardless of whether `BINSIM_URL` was set. The launcher pre-dates the binsim work (the MAJOR-001 paper/live URL safety wall was built when "paper" meant "paper orders to Binance Spot testnet"); when binsim wiring landed (Praxis [#112](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/112)) the spec-prescribed URL override at [`launcher.py:1873-1875`](praxis/launcher.py) covered the trading-path REST/WS/WS-API triple but **the market-data client constructed separately at [`launcher.py:1294`](praxis/launcher.py) inherited the `testnet=True` flag from the paper-mode default**. Production observation on `praxis:0.68.1` with `BINSIM_URL=http://binsim:8081`: every `binancial` cache-refresh call hit `https://testnet.binance.vision/api/v3/aggTrades` and the `historicalTrades` GETs intermittently raised `ValueError: No trades found at or after <ts>` because testnet has no continuous trade flow. Sensors built their feature reconstruction from sparse testnet trades while binsim filled against its mainnet-quality depth feed — defeating the purpose of running the sim. The Praxis [#112](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/112) spec is explicit ("Removes testnet-borne incidents... and unlocks the same backend for high-speed replay later" and the order-book source is mainnet-mirrored at 1s cadence), so the coupling was an unintended gap, not a deliberate constraint

### Fix

- Route market-data poller to mainnet (`testnet=False`) when `BINSIM_URL` is set under `TRADE_MODE=paper` in [`_resolve_trade_mode`](praxis/launcher.py). The trading-path URL triple still derives from `BINSIM_URL`; only the fourth tuple element flips. The MAJOR-001 safety property (no paper-orders against mainnet data) is preserved because the property only meaningfully applies when paper orders reach a real venue — binsim orders never do. `TRADE_MODE=paper` without `BINSIM_URL` is unchanged (returns testnet URLs + `testnet=True`); `TRADE_MODE=live` is unchanged (mainnet + `testnet=False`); `TRADE_MODE=live` with `BINSIM_URL` set still raises (unchanged). Update the docstring on `_resolve_trade_mode` and the env-routing table in [`docs/Binsim.md`](docs/Binsim.md) to document the mainnet market-data path under binsim
- Update [`TestResolveTradeModeBinsim`](tests/test_launcher_trade_mode.py): the two assertions that previously pinned `testnet is True` under `paper+BINSIM_URL` (`test_paper_with_binsim_url_returns_derived_urls_and_testnet_flag`, `test_paper_with_https_binsim_url_uses_wss`) now assert `testnet is False` and have been renamed to `test_paper_with_binsim_url_returns_derived_urls_and_mainnet_market_data` / `test_paper_with_https_binsim_url_uses_wss_and_mainnet_market_data` with a docstring stating the rationale (binsim's mainnet-quality depth feed plus sensor feature reconstruction must read the same data distribution); empty/whitespace `BINSIM_URL` fallback to testnet+`True` is unchanged

## v0.69.0 on 31st of May, 2026

### NOTE

- Wires the v0.54.0 Nexus schedulers ([`SnapshotScheduler`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/blob/bd61a0a60eefe8c55ef43719c72081193f66e097/nexus/infrastructure/snapshot_scheduler.py) + [`MtmLoop`](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/blob/bd61a0a60eefe8c55ef43719c72081193f66e097/nexus/core/mtm_loop.py)) into [`praxis/launcher.py`](praxis/launcher.py) so the v0.54.0 fixes for Nexus [#76](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/issues/76) actually take effect on prod. Pre-v0.69.0, the Nexus modules existed but the launcher never instantiated them, so `state_store.checkpoint()` still ran only at boot-time capital reconciliation and graceful shutdown (snapshot drifted 13+ h behind the live state on the observed prod box, WAL grew to 11.5 GB) and `Position.unrealized_pnl` stayed at zero across the lifetime of every position (live MTM hid +$3,066 unrealized across 4,161 open positions on the observed prod box, with risk gates running blind to the open book). This pin bump + wiring delivers both fixes to the running deployment

### Add

- Wire [`SnapshotScheduler`](praxis/launcher.py) into the per-account Nexus runtime in [`Launcher._build_nexus_runtime`](praxis/launcher.py) alongside `HealthLoop`. Constructed with `positions_lock` (the same `threading.Lock` already aliased into `state.risk.lock` for the FINAL-MAJOR-02 cross-thread invariant), `capital_lock_cm=capital_controller.lock_cm`, and `interval_seconds` resolved from `NEXUS_SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default 300 s). Started before the runtime is returned; stopped in `_run_nexus_instance` BEFORE `ShutdownSequencer` runs its own final checkpoint, so the scheduler's last periodic checkpoint and the shutdown checkpoint cannot race. Lock-cluster invariant is enforced by the scheduler's constructor (PR [#77](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/pull/77) review): both `state.risk.lock is positions_lock` AND `capital_lock_cm` must be supplied when `positions_lock` is — Praxis's wiring satisfies both conditions
- Wire [`MtmLoop`](praxis/launcher.py) into the same per-account Nexus runtime. Constructed with `positions_lock` (same identity-equal lock as above) and a `mark_price_provider` callable that returns `Decimal | None` per symbol: for the current single-symbol BTCUSDT deployment, it wraps the existing [`_last_close_from_poller`](praxis/launcher.py) (which returns the last 1m close from [`MainCache`](praxis/market_data_cache.py) or `None` when the cache is empty or stale). Symbols other than `BTCUSDT` return `None`, which `MtmLoop` treats as "mark unavailable" and aborts the tick without partial writes. `interval_seconds` resolved from `NEXUS_MTM_INTERVAL_SECONDS` (default 30 s). Started after `SnapshotScheduler.start()`; stopped before it in `_run_nexus_instance`
- Add `snapshot_scheduler: SnapshotScheduler` and `mtm_loop: MtmLoop` fields to the [`_NexusRuntime`](praxis/launcher.py) dataclass so `_run_nexus_instance` can stop both in lifecycle order before `ShutdownSequencer`. Add `_DEFAULT_SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 300.0` and `_DEFAULT_MTM_INTERVAL_SECONDS = 30.0` module constants
- Add [`_positive_float_env`](praxis/launcher.py) helper that parses a positive, **finite** numeric env var with operator-visible errors on typo or invalid value. Bare `float(os.environ.get(...))` raises `ValueError: could not convert string to float: ...` which surfaces as a per-account thread death (caught by `_run_nexus_instance`'s BLE001) with no operator-actionable signal; the helper validates the value and raises `RuntimeError` carrying the env var name + value + expected shape, which surfaces in the launcher's structured log. The finiteness gate (`math.isfinite(value)`) rejects `inf` and `nan` — both slip past `value <= 0` (`float('inf') <= 0` and `float('nan') <= 0` are both `False`), and either would propagate to `threading.Timer` as silent misconfiguration (`inf` makes the scheduler wait forever, `nan` is undefined) — defeating the helper's whole purpose of operator-visible validation (PR [#128](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/128) review by zero-bang). Applied to `NEXUS_SNAPSHOT_INTERVAL_SECONDS` and `NEXUS_MTM_INTERVAL_SECONDS`. Tests in [`tests/test_launcher_positive_float_env.py`](tests/test_launcher_positive_float_env.py) (9 cases) pin unset/empty/valid/non-numeric/zero/negative/`inf`/`nan` paths
- Add TD-069 to [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md): `MtmLoop.mark_price_provider` returns `None` for any symbol other than `BTCUSDT`, which `MtmLoop` treats as "abort entire tick" (per its strict-no-partial-writes contract). Low impact today (BTCUSDT-only deployments shipped) but becomes a correctness blocker on first multi-symbol manifest — silently degrades MTM for the open book with only a per-tick WARN log as signal. Migration path: per-symbol-keyed `MainCache.get_last_close(symbol, kline_size)` plus a boot-time assertion that every manifest symbol has a working lookup

### Update

- Bump `vaquum-nexus` pin from `2cae5c0` (v0.53.1) to `bd61a0a` ([Nexus PR #77](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/pull/77), v0.54.0) in [`pyproject.toml`](pyproject.toml). v0.54.0 ships `SnapshotScheduler` + `MtmLoop` + a `strategy_unrealized_pnl: Decimal` field on `StrategyRiskState` + split `max_drawdown` (realized-only) vs new `max_total_drawdown` (realized + unrealized) lifetime peaks on `RiskState`. Backward-compat decode (`_decode_risk_state` defaults `max_total_drawdown` and `_decode_strategy_risk_state` defaults `strategy_unrealized_pnl` to `'0'` when missing) means existing Praxis snapshots load without migration; the new MtmLoop tick overwrites zeros on the next cycle. The validator's max-drawdown gate (`risk_stage.py` → `to_risk_check_metrics` → `max_drawdown_pct`) retains realized-only semantics — pre-v0.69.0 calibration stays valid; the new `max_total_drawdown_pct` field is exposed for telemetry and any future tighter mark-aware gate
- Update [`tests/test_launcher_shutdown_config_wiring.py`](tests/test_launcher_shutdown_config_wiring.py) `_stub_nexus_runtime` to supply the two new `_NexusRuntime` fields (`snapshot_scheduler=MagicMock()`, `mtm_loop=MagicMock()`) so the shutdown-wiring test still constructs a complete runtime. Praxis's full pytest suite passes against the new stack: **1354 passed, 1 skipped** — identical to v0.68.2 baseline

## v0.70.0 on 2nd of June, 2026

### NOTE

- Hardens the binsim depth poller against a recurring upstream-mirror failure mode. Post-mortem of the 2026-05-31T16:47:23Z HALT (recovered manually, ran for 19h+ in HALTED state before restart) traced the cascade to a single bad upstream snapshot served by [`binance-spot-depth20-1000ms.onrender.com`](https://binance-spot-depth20-1000ms.onrender.com/top20): 17 consecutive `POST /api/v3/order` rejections over 629 ms all reported `insufficient book liquidity: requested 0.02721, visible 0.00962 across 20 levels` — the exact same `visible` value to the last decimal across all 17. That uniformity is the design's intended in-window behavior (`OrderBook.consume_qty_for_market_order` is non-mutating, so all orders within a single 1000 ms poll window see the identical snapshot — see [`praxis/binsim/book.py`](praxis/binsim/book.py) docstring), but a top-20 BTCUSDT depth of 0.00962 BTC is not a real Binance state; the upstream mirror served broken data and binsim accepted it because no policy gate beyond shape validation existed. The same identical-`visible` cluster pattern appears in the event spine for May 27 (2 hits @ 0.02141/0.02149), May 28 (3 hits across hours), May 30 morning (9 hits @ 0.02023 in 0.45 s), and May 30 evening (2 hits @ 0.02550 in 5 ms) — recurring mirror pathology, not isolated market events. The fixes below add two policy gates and one diagnostic at the poller boundary so future hiccups produce clean `-1003 (book stale)` rejections via the existing HTTP staleness gate rather than burst rejections that look like market signal to `HealthEvaluator`

### Add

- Add a magnitude-floor gate to [`DepthPoller.poll_once`](praxis/binsim/feed.py): on each successful HTTP poll, after `_parse_payload` succeeds, compute `sum(qty for _, qty in asks[:20])` and `sum(qty for _, qty in bids[:20])`. If either is below `min_top20_depth_btc` (default `0.05`, env-tunable via `BINSIM_MIN_TOP20_DEPTH_BTC`), log at ERROR with both sums + per-side `*_levels_in_payload` counts + the floor + `last_update_id` + `ts_ms` and return without touching the book or `_last_success_ts_ms`. The book stays on its last accepted snapshot; the existing 5 s HTTP staleness gate in [`server._submit_order`](praxis/binsim/server.py) fires after the threshold and surfaces the upstream problem as Binance-shaped `-1003` rejections. The 0.05 BTC default catches every historical bad snapshot (`0.00962 → 0.02597`) while leaving headroom over the current upstream bid-side depth (~0.4 BTC). The check is part of the policy layer, not `OrderBook.replace`, so `OrderBook` stays a pure data-shape primitive. The module constant `_TOP_20_LEVELS = 20` (Greybeard pre-PR review) keeps the gate width's "20" tied to the env var name `BINSIM_MIN_TOP20_DEPTH_BTC` so the two cannot drift
- Add a stuck-`lastUpdateId` detector to [`DepthPoller.poll_once`](praxis/binsim/feed.py): real Binance advances `lastUpdateId` on every diff, so an upstream serving the identical id across `max_stuck_update_id_polls` consecutive polls (default `5`, env-tunable via `BINSIM_MAX_STUCK_UPDATE_ID_POLLS`, **meaningful minimum `2`** — baseline + one repeat; `1` would brick the feed since the first poll alone would trip the threshold, so both the `DepthPoller` constructor and the env-var parser reject it explicitly — PR [#129](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/129) review by zero-bang / Copilot) is frozen even if its shape and magnitude validate. New `_previous_last_update_id` + `_stuck_update_id_count` poller state tracks the run, counting the **first observation as poll 1** so `BINSIM_MAX_STUCK_UPDATE_ID_POLLS=N` means "reject on the Nth consecutive identical poll" — matches the operator's natural reading of the env-var name (Greybeard pre-PR review). A fresh id resets the counter to 1 (this poll is observation 1 of the new id); reaching the threshold logs at ERROR with the id + run length + threshold and returns without replace / `_last_success_ts_ms` update. Same recovery path as the magnitude floor — the HTTP staleness gate becomes the operator's surfacing layer
- Add a per-poll success diagnostic log to [`DepthPoller.poll_once`](praxis/binsim/feed.py): on every successful `book.replace`, emit a single structured INFO line containing `ask_top_n_qty`, `bid_top_n_qty`, `ask_levels_in_payload`, `bid_levels_in_payload`, `best_ask_price`, `best_ask_qty`, `best_bid_price`, `best_bid_qty`, `last_update_id`, `ts_ms`. The `*_levels_in_payload` fields (Greybeard pre-PR review) record how many levels the upstream actually returned so a truncated snapshot (e.g. 3 levels totalling 0.06 BTC) cannot pass the floor silently — the operator can spot from logs that the depth was thin-by-truncation rather than thin-by-market. Previously the success path was silent — only failures logged — which made post-mortem reconstruction of bad upstream snapshots impossible once the affected `OrderSubmitFailed` events rotated out of the launcher's container log
- Add env-var wiring in [`praxis/binsim/__main__.py`](praxis/binsim/__main__.py): two new optional `BINSIM_*` env vars (`BINSIM_MIN_TOP20_DEPTH_BTC`, `BINSIM_MAX_STUCK_UPDATE_ID_POLLS`) feed `_Config` and the `DepthPoller` constructor. The Decimal parser (`_parse_positive_decimal_env`) rejects non-finite values (`NaN`, `Infinity`, `-Infinity`), zero, and negatives at parse time so the validation surfaces before the poller starts. The `binsim ready` startup log line includes both tunables so deployment operators see the configured values without inspecting env vars manually. Both new env vars are also documented in [`docs/Binsim.md`](docs/Binsim.md)'s operator-facing "Required Binsim Environment" reference table so operators consult one canonical source (PR [#129](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/129) review by zero-bang)
- Add 11 new tests to [`tests/test_binsim_feed.py`](tests/test_binsim_feed.py) covering: constructor validation for non-positive / non-finite `min_top20_depth_btc` and non-positive `max_stuck_update_id_polls`; `poll_once` rejection paths for sub-floor ask-side and bid-side depth; `poll_once` acceptance at exact floor; stuck-id detector rejecting after `N` identical polls; stuck-id counter resetting when the id changes; and the per-poll success diagnostic containing the magnitude + best-of-book values needed by an operator post-mortem. Add 4 new tests to [`tests/test_binsim_main.py`](tests/test_binsim_main.py) for the new env vars (defaults, full overrides, invalid-decimal cases including `NaN`/`Infinity`/`0`/negative, invalid-int cases for the stuck-polls var, blank-fallback)

### Update

- Bump `version` from `0.69.0` to `0.70.0` in [`pyproject.toml`](pyproject.toml). Full binsim test suite passes: **275 tests passed** across `test_binsim_book.py`, `test_binsim_feed.py` (54 cases, 11 new), `test_binsim_main.py` (37 cases, 4 new), `test_binsim_server.py`, `test_binsim_ledger.py`, `test_binsim_ws.py`. `ruff check` clean on the four touched files
- Note TD-070 in [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md): the per-poll success diagnostic INFO log fires at the full `BINSIM_POLL_INTERVAL_MS` cadence (~86,400 lines/day at the 1 Hz default), which is operationally noisy enough to push useful Praxis events past the container `json-file` driver's `max-size=50m × max-file=5` rotation window faster than they would otherwise age out. Migration: either throttle the INFO line to a once-per-N-polls heartbeat with the per-poll line at DEBUG, or emit the snapshot as a structured metric instead. When to fix: when the volume starts displacing useful Praxis logs in a real post-mortem, OR when a metrics path lands
- Note TD-071 in [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md): the magnitude floor applies `min_top20_depth_btc` symmetrically to ask AND bid sides via `or`, but the live upstream shows persistent ask/bid asymmetry (~5.6 BTC ask vs ~0.4 BTC bid top-20) and the current buy-only deployment depends on ask-side liquidity; any future tightening of the floor past the upstream's typical bid depth would force every poll to reject on the bid side even with a healthy ask. Migration: split into `min_top20_ask_depth_btc` + `min_top20_bid_depth_btc` poller fields with corresponding env vars, defaulting to the existing single-knob value for backward compatibility. When to fix: when an incident reveals ask-side thinness in the gap between the current floor and the bid-side norm, OR when a sell-side strategy lands

## v0.71.0 on 4th of June, 2026

### NOTE

- Adds a Docker-Compose-based observability stack (ClickHouse + spine-mirror service + Grafana) under [`observability/`](observability/) that ingests every row from `event_spine.sqlite` into a `ReplacingMergeTree(event_seq)` ClickHouse table for flexible querying. Mirrors against `/opt/praxis/state/event_spine.sqlite` read-only and tails new `event_seq` rows on a 5-second cadence; ClickHouse `MATERIALIZED` columns project `strategy_id`, `command_id`, `trade_id`, `symbol`, `side`, `account_id`, `status`, `reason`, `qty`, `price`, `fee` out of the JSON payload at write time so dashboards can filter / group / aggregate without per-query `JSONExtract*`. Grafana is provisioned with the ClickHouse datasource + two starter dashboards (`praxis-overview` and `praxis-events`) and exposes UI on `127.0.0.1:3001` behind basic auth. ClickHouse exposes two operator-facing ports: HTTP on `127.0.0.1:8124` for ad-hoc `curl http://praxis:<PW>@127.0.0.1:8124/?query=...` style access, and the native TCP protocol on `127.0.0.1:9001` for `clickhouse-client --host 127.0.0.1 --port 9001 --user praxis --password ... --database praxis` (the native protocol is what `clickhouse-client` uses; HTTP is for tooling that prefers REST). The stack is operationally separate from Praxis runtime: stopping it has zero impact on trading

### Add

- Add [`observability/docker-compose.observability.yml`](observability/docker-compose.observability.yml) — three services on a private `observability` bridge network: `praxis-clickhouse` (ClickHouse 24.8, persistent named volume `clickhouse_data`, file-descriptor `ulimit` 262144, published on `127.0.0.1:8124`), `praxis-spine-mirror` (built from [`observability/Dockerfile.spine-mirror`](observability/Dockerfile.spine-mirror) — `python:3.12-slim` base with `clickhouse-connect==0.8.10` pre-installed and `spine_mirror.py` baked in at image build, no pip-install on container start; mounts the host's `/opt/praxis/state` read-only), and `praxis-grafana` (Grafana 11.2.0, `grafana-clickhouse-datasource` plugin auto-installed at start, anonymous + sign-up disabled, basic-auth required, published on `127.0.0.1:3001`). Both secrets (`CLICKHOUSE_PASSWORD`, `GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD`) are read from an `observability/.env` file via Compose env interpolation with mandatory `:?<msg>` guards so the stack refuses to start with missing values. Add [`observability/Dockerfile.spine-mirror`](observability/Dockerfile.spine-mirror) so spine-mirror builds a pre-baked image rather than running `pip install` on every container start
- Add [`observability/spine_mirror.py`](observability/spine_mirror.py) — standalone polling-mirror service. Opens the spine SQLite as `file:...?mode=ro` via a `with sqlite3.connect(...)` context manager (URI mode with `PRAGMA query_only=ON` for defense-in-depth read-only access), tracks the high-water mark via `SELECT max(event_seq) FROM <database>.events` (idempotent on restart — no separate cursor file; `<database>` is the validated `CLICKHOUSE_DATABASE` value), pulls up to `BATCH_SIZE=10000` rows per tick `WHERE event_seq > cursor ORDER BY event_seq`, parses the `timestamp` column to a UTC-aware `datetime` via `_parse_ts` (handles `+00:00`, `Z`, naive, and tz-aware non-UTC offsets — uses `astimezone(UTC)` when an offset is present and only `replace(tzinfo=UTC)` for truly-naive inputs), and batch-inserts into `<database>.events`. Recoverable errors are caught via `_RECOVERABLE_ERRORS = (sqlite3.Error, OSError, clickhouse_connect.driver.exceptions.DatabaseError, clickhouse_connect.driver.exceptions.OperationalError)`, logged at ERROR with traceback via `_log.exception`, and sleeping is gated by `_backoff_seconds(consecutive_failures)` (exponential, doubling from 1s, clamped at 300s) so a transient ClickHouse outage / network blip does not kill the service — the next successful tick reads the persisted `max(event_seq)` and resumes
- Add [`observability/grafana-provisioning/datasources/clickhouse.yaml`](observability/grafana-provisioning/datasources/clickhouse.yaml) — auto-provisions the "Praxis ClickHouse" datasource (UID `praxis-clickhouse`) at Grafana start; password is read from the `CLICKHOUSE_PASSWORD` env var that Compose injects into the Grafana container
- Add [`observability/grafana-provisioning/dashboards/dashboards.yaml`](observability/grafana-provisioning/dashboards/dashboards.yaml) + [`praxis-overview.json`](observability/grafana-provisioning/dashboards/praxis-overview.json) + [`praxis-events.json`](observability/grafana-provisioning/dashboards/praxis-events.json) — two starter dashboards available immediately on first launch. `praxis-overview` shows event-rate-per-type, top-N strategies by event count, and fill / reject / ack proportions; `praxis-events` is a row-level query workbench with `strategy_id` / `event_type` / time-range variables wired to the materialized columns
- Add [`observability/.env.example`](observability/.env.example) — commit-safe template with placeholder values for `CLICKHOUSE_PASSWORD` and `GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD`. Operators copy this to `observability/.env` (already gitignored via the repo-wide `.env` rule) and fill in the real values before `docker compose -f observability/docker-compose.observability.yml up -d --build` (`--build` is required on first launch to bake the spine-mirror image)

### Update

- Bump `version` from `0.70.0` to `0.71.0` in [`pyproject.toml`](pyproject.toml). The observability stack is operational infrastructure — no Praxis runtime code is touched, no Praxis test surface is added or modified; existing test suites continue to pass unchanged
- Pre-PR Greybeard review (6 VALID_FIX, 5 VALID_TD recorded; describes the diff from the first commit's pre-fix draft to the shipped behaviour): hoist env-var reads from module scope into `main()` so the module can be imported for testing / tooling without the full prod env set; replace the draft's three `print()` call sites with `logging.getLogger(__name__)` + `_log.info` / `_log.exception` (the latter auto-includes a traceback so a future ClickHouse driver `DatabaseError` lands in the container log with full call frames); narrow the draft's bare `except Exception` to a `_RECOVERABLE_ERRORS = (sqlite3.Error, OSError, clickhouse_connect.driver.exceptions.DatabaseError, clickhouse_connect.driver.exceptions.OperationalError)` tuple so programming errors propagate immediately instead of being swallowed; add exponential backoff — the shipped function is `_backoff_seconds(consecutive_failures)` (single arg after the round-1 review simplified the original draft signature), doubling from `_BACKOFF_BASE_S=1.0s` per consecutive failure and clamping at `_BACKOFF_MAX_S=300s`, with the counter resetting to `0` on the next successful tick (replaces the draft's "hot-loop every 5s on ClickHouse outage" pattern that would have hammered a broken upstream indefinitely); rename the `_to_rows` loop variable from `payload` to `raw_payload` + introduce `decoded_payload` so the rebinding-loop-var pattern flagged by ruff `PLW2901` is gone; use `datetime.UTC` instead of the deprecated `datetime.timezone.utc` (ruff `UP017`). Also: convert the spine-mirror container from the draft's `image: python:3.12-slim` + `command: sh -c "pip install ... && python ..."` (which ran `pip install` on every container start, 5–10s of network-dependent latency, fails on a flaky pip mirror) to a pre-baked image built from [`observability/Dockerfile.spine-mirror`](observability/Dockerfile.spine-mirror) + `image: praxis-spine-mirror:0.71.0`. `ruff check` clean on the touched file
- Pre-PR Greybeard review (deferred to TD): record TD-073, TD-074, TD-075, TD-076 in [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md). TD-073: `clickhouse_connect.get_client` is called once at startup and the library's internal auto-reconnect on transport failure is documented but not verified end-to-end against a real ClickHouse container restart; migration is a `_ClientHolder` that rebuilds on consecutive `OperationalError`. TD-074: `depends_on.praxis-clickhouse.condition: service_started` waits for the container to be up, not for ClickHouse to accept queries; migration is a healthcheck block on `praxis-clickhouse` and flipping both consumers to `service_healthy`. TD-075: the `/opt/praxis/state:/spine:ro` bind-mount source is a hardcoded host path; migration is `${PRAXIS_STATE_DIR:-/opt/praxis/state}` with the default preserving existing deployments. TD-076: `spine_mirror.py`'s `_parse_ts`, `_current_cursor`, `_backoff_seconds`, and `_to_rows` have no automated coverage; migration is a `tests/test_spine_mirror.py` with parser-shape / NULL-cursor / backoff-clamp cases
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#131](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/131) round-1 review by zero-bang + Copilot, 13 threads): (1) pin Grafana to `127.0.0.1:3001:3000` in [`docker-compose.observability.yml`](observability/docker-compose.observability.yml) — was `"3001:3000"` which binds all interfaces, contradicting the documented localhost-only posture (CHANGELOG NOTE bullet); (2) drop the `action_type` MATERIALIZED column from the ClickHouse schema in [`spine_mirror.py`](observability/spine_mirror.py) — no spine event dataclass carries an `action_type` field (audited all 12 events in [`praxis/core/domain/events.py`](praxis/core/domain/events.py); `action_type` is a Nexus `ActionType` attribute that lives on `nexus.strategy.action.Action`, not on any persisted Praxis spine event) so the column was permanently empty `''` and the `open_pos` panel's `countIf(event_type='CommandAccepted' AND action_type='enter')` was always `0`; the panel's query is rewritten to `uniqExactIf(trade_id, event_type='CommandAccepted' AND trade_id != '') - uniqExactIf(trade_id, event_type='TradeClosed' AND trade_id != '')` which correctly counts open trades via distinct `trade_id` (each closed trade emits 2 `CommandAccepted` events for entry+exit but only 1 `TradeClosed`); `action_type` references also dropped from [`praxis-events.json`](observability/grafana-provisioning/dashboards/praxis-events.json)'s `SELECT` and column-width override; (3) parameterize the `_SCHEMA_SQL` table name via `_SCHEMA_SQL_TEMPLATE.format(database=database)` so a non-default `CLICKHOUSE_DATABASE` env value doesn't create the table in `praxis` and then insert into the configured database; (4) hoist `clickhouse_connect.get_client` + `_ensure_schema` into the retry loop body, gated by `ch is None` and `schema_ready` flags, so a ClickHouse cold-start race (the `depends_on: service_started` only-waits-for-container-up case) flows through `_RECOVERABLE_ERRORS` + `_backoff_seconds` instead of crashing the process before the loop starts; (5) simplify `_backoff_seconds(consecutive_failures)` — drop the unused `base_interval_s` parameter and the dead `consecutive_failures <= 0` branch (the call site always passes `>= 1` after the `consecutive_failures += 1` increment); update the call site accordingly; (6) switch `_fetch_batch` to `with sqlite3.connect(...) as conn:` context manager (consistent with [`tests/test_event_spine.py`](tests/test_event_spine.py)'s usage); (7) switch the three `MATERIALIZED toDecimal128OrZero` columns (`qty`, `price`, `fee`) to `Nullable(Decimal(38, 18))` + `toDecimal128OrNull` (Copilot review overruled the pre-PR TD-072 deferral: brand-new stack, no migration needed — TD-072 entry is removed);
