(8) wrap the cash-flow panel's `qty * price ± fee` arithmetic in `coalesce(col, 0)` so the newly-Nullable columns don't propagate `NULL` through the time series; (9) `password: $CLICKHOUSE_PASSWORD` → `password: ${CLICKHOUSE_PASSWORD}` in [`grafana-provisioning/datasources/clickhouse.yaml`](observability/grafana-provisioning/datasources/clickhouse.yaml) — Grafana provisioning expands `${VAR}` but treats `$VAR` as a literal, so the datasource was authenticating with the string `"$CLICKHOUSE_PASSWORD"` (would have failed silently against ClickHouse on first dashboard load); (10) remove the hardcoded `allValue: "0,1,...,20"` from the `epoch` variable in [`praxis-overview.json`](observability/grafana-provisioning/dashboards/praxis-overview.json) — once `epoch_id` exceeds 20, selecting "All" would silently exclude newer epochs; with `allValue` omitted Grafana fans out the variable's current query results dynamically; (11) update the original `Add` bullet for [`docker-compose.observability.yml`](observability/docker-compose.observability.yml) and the `Add` bullet for [`.env.example`](observability/.env.example) above to reflect the actual shipped stack (pre-baked Dockerfile image, `127.0.0.1:3001`, `observability/`-prefixed compose-up example with `--build`)
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#131](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/131) round-2 review by Copilot, 6 threads): (1) validate `CLICKHOUSE_DATABASE` against a `^[A-Za-z_][A-Za-z0-9_]*$` identifier-shape regex in `main()` before it gets interpolated into the schema `CREATE DATABASE` / `CREATE TABLE` SQL strings — rejects names with quoting needs, whitespace, hyphens, or anything malicious before the first SQL call lands; (2) NOTE bullet for v0.71.0 above no longer claims an `action_type` MATERIALIZED column (dropped in round-1, the NOTE list is now in sync with the shipped schema); (3) the `Add` bullet for `docker-compose.observability.yml` above no longer says "All three secrets" — there are two (`CLICKHOUSE_PASSWORD`, `GRAFANA_ADMIN_PASSWORD`); (4 + 5) the Pre-PR Greybeard `Update` bullet above is reframed to make clear the `_backoff_seconds(consecutive_failures, base_interval_s)` and bare-`except Exception` shapes are the **draft** state being replaced, not the shipped state; the shipped function is `_backoff_seconds(consecutive_failures)` and the shipped error scope is the `_RECOVERABLE_ERRORS` tuple; (6) TD-074 description in [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md) updated to reference the shipped `_RECOVERABLE_ERRORS` retry envelope and the `_backoff_seconds(consecutive_failures)` API instead of the round-0 draft signature
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#131](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/131) round-3 review by Copilot, 2 threads): (1) `_parse_ts` in [`observability/spine_mirror.py`](observability/spine_mirror.py) no longer overwrites the offset on tz-aware non-UTC strings — the previous implementation's default branch used `datetime.fromisoformat(raw).replace(tzinfo=UTC)`, which silently shifted instants when the spine wrote `event.timestamp.isoformat()` with any offset other than `+00:00`. The spine's validator (`_EventBase.__post_init__`) enforces tz-awareness but not UTC, so a non-UTC offset can land on disk; the fix normalizes a trailing `Z` to `+00:00`, parses via `fromisoformat`, and then either `astimezone(UTC)` (preserves instant) when the parsed value is tz-aware OR `replace(tzinfo=UTC)` only for the truly-naive case. Verified against six shapes (`+00:00`, `Z`, naive, `-05:00`, `+05:30`, microsecond-bearing) via inline smoke-tests; (2) add docstrings to all seven functions in [`spine_mirror.py`](observability/spine_mirror.py) (`_parse_ts`, `_ensure_schema`, `_current_cursor`, `_fetch_batch`, `_to_rows`, `_backoff_seconds`, `main`) matching the project's NumPy-style convention used in [`praxis/infrastructure/observability.py`](praxis/infrastructure/observability.py) and [`praxis/launcher.py`](praxis/launcher.py) (one-line summary, `Args:`, `Returns:`/`Raises:` blocks where applicable, single-quote triple-strings, no embedded examples per CLAUDE.md)
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#131](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/131) round-4 review by Copilot, 6 threads): (1) fix the NOTE bullet's ClickHouse-access wording — was "ClickHouse HTTP on `127.0.0.1:8124` for ad-hoc psql / `clickhouse-client` access" which is wrong on two counts (ClickHouse doesn't use `psql`; `clickhouse-client` uses the native TCP protocol on `127.0.0.1:9001`, not HTTP). New wording splits the two access paths: HTTP for `curl http://praxis:<PW>@127.0.0.1:8124/?query=...` style REST tooling, native TCP for `clickhouse-client --port 9001`; (2) rewrite the `Add observability/spine_mirror.py` bullet (line 876) to describe the shipped behaviour — replaces the stale draft description of "Bare-`Exception` catch + structured `print(...)`" with the actual shipped `_RECOVERABLE_ERRORS = (sqlite3.Error, OSError, clickhouse_connect.driver.exceptions.DatabaseError, clickhouse_connect.driver.exceptions.OperationalError)` tuple, `_log.exception` logging, exponential backoff via `_backoff_seconds(consecutive_failures)`, `with sqlite3.connect(...)` context manager, validated `<database>` identifier, and the `_parse_ts` astimezone-vs-replace branching. The line-871 NOTE-bullet fix and this line-876 Add-bullet fix together close the last two CHANGELOG sites that still described the round-0 draft instead of the shipped v0.71.0 state; (3 – 6) switch all four `praxis-events.json` panels (rows-matching, distinct-strategies, distinct-commands, spine-events-table) from raw-interpolated `'$strategy_id_filter'` to Grafana's `${strategy_id_filter:sqlstring}` formatter, which auto-quotes the value and escapes embedded single quotes via the SQL `''` doubling convention. This closes the SQL-injection / quote-breakage surface in the events dashboard for any operator input containing a `'` (e.g. a strategy_id like `O'Brien_v3`). `praxis-overview.json` does not declare or interpolate `strategy_id_filter` so no parallel sites; the SELECT clauses' static single-quoted literals (`event_type = 'CommandAccepted'`, etc.) are not affected — only operator-provided variables route through `:sqlstring`
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#131](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/131) round-5 review by Copilot, 4 threads): (1) harden [`observability/Dockerfile.spine-mirror`](observability/Dockerfile.spine-mirror) to match the repo's existing image-build pattern in [`Dockerfile`](Dockerfile) + [`Dockerfile.binsim`](Dockerfile.binsim) — add `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` + `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` + `PIP_NO_CACHE_DIR=1` + `PIP_DISABLE_PIP_VERSION_CHECK=1` env defaults, create a non-root `spinemirror` user at `uid 1000` via `useradd --create-home`, `chown -R spinemirror:spinemirror /app`, and `USER spinemirror` so the spine-mirror process drops privileges before running `spine_mirror.py`. Reduces blast radius if the process is ever compromised (the mount of `/opt/praxis/state` is already `:ro` so the worst case is a non-root container with read-only access to the spine — no write path to anything on the host); (2 + 3) replace the hardcoded `"current": {"selected": true, "text": "10", "value": "10"}` default on the `epoch` variable in both [`praxis-overview.json`](observability/grafana-provisioning/dashboards/praxis-overview.json) and [`praxis-events.json`](observability/grafana-provisioning/dashboards/praxis-events.json) with `"current": {"selected": true, "text": "All", "value": "$__all"}` so a fresh / new-host deployment opens to the full epoch set instead of an empty dashboard (the `"10"` was a copy from the dev box's epoch); (4) cache the spine cursor in-process across polling ticks in [`spine_mirror.py`](observability/spine_mirror.py)'s `main()`. The previous implementation called `_current_cursor(ch, ch_database)` on every tick — a `SELECT max(event_seq) FROM <database>.events` round-trip against ClickHouse — even though the mirror already knows the last inserted `event_seq` after each successful insert. The new flow primes `cached_cursor` from `_current_cursor` exactly once on the first successful tick (logged at INFO: `'cursor primed from ClickHouse: event_seq=%d'`), advances it to `last_seq` after each successful `ch.insert`, and only re-primes from ClickHouse on a process restart. Steady-state ticks now hit only the SQLite `_fetch_batch` query and (when there are new rows) the ClickHouse `INSERT`; the read-side `max(event_seq)` query is gone from the hot path. Per-tick latency drops from "two ClickHouse round-trips + one SQLite query" to "one SQLite query" when the spine is idle, and "two ClickHouse round-trips + one SQLite query" → "one ClickHouse round-trip + one SQLite query" when there are new rows. `main()`'s docstring updated to document the cursor-priming + advance-on-insert semantics
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#131](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/131) round-6 review by Copilot, 2 threads): (1) drop the `database=ch_database` keyword from `clickhouse_connect.get_client(...)` in [`spine_mirror.py`](observability/spine_mirror.py) `main()`. The client constructor selects the named database on connect; if the target database doesn't yet exist (which is exactly the case `_ensure_schema`'s `CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS` is meant to handle), the very first `get_client` call would have failed with "Database doesn't exist" — putting `main()` into a permanent `_RECOVERABLE_ERRORS` backoff loop, never reaching `_ensure_schema` to create the database. The fix removes `database=` so the connection is database-less; every SQL statement already uses fully-qualified `<database>.events` so the default-database selection was not load-bearing for any query path. Mirror can now bootstrap against a ClickHouse instance that has never seen the target database; (2) add [`tests/test_spine_mirror.py`](tests/test_spine_mirror.py) (36 cases, all pass) covering `_parse_ts` across 6 timestamp shapes (UTC, Z, naive, `-05:00`, `+05:30`, microsecond-bearing), `_backoff_seconds` monotonicity + clamp at `_BACKOFF_MAX_S`, `_to_rows` bytes/str/invalid-utf8 round-trip + integer coercion, `_current_cursor` empty-table / NULL / populated / supplied-database paths against a mocked `clickhouse_connect` client, `_IDENTIFIER_RE` positive (6 safe names) + negative (10 unsafe names including hyphen / space / semicolon / `'` / `` ` `` / `$` / `.` / SQL-injection shapes), and `_ensure_schema` issues the correct `CREATE DATABASE` + `CREATE TABLE` statements against the supplied database name. Test file uses `importlib.util.spec_from_file_location` to load the module by path since `observability/spine_mirror.py` is outside the `praxis` package import surface. TD-076 entry removed from [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md) (Copilot review overruled the pre-PR deferral, matching the TD-072 pattern from round-1)

## v0.72.0 on 5th of June, 2026

### NOTE

- Closes [Vaquum/Praxis#130](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/130). Companion fix to [Vaquum/Nexus#78](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/issues/78) (terminal-fill residual release), which closed with [Vaquum/Nexus#79](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/pull/79) merged at Nexus `ac0aadd`. Same prod incident class — 876 strategies × ~\$2–7 of ghost-deployed capital after fully-closed round-trips on Praxis prod (epoch 10, binsim) — split between the Praxis-side qty mismatch and the Nexus-side notional residual. Nexus v0.55.0 closed the notional half; this PR closes the qty half by floor-snapping `cmd.qty` to the venue's `LOT_SIZE.stepSize` at command-creation time (before the validator pipeline runs capital reservation), so `TradeOutcomeProduced.status=PARTIAL` no longer fires on every entry where the strategy's un-quantized `action.size = budget / reference_price` carried sub-step precision. Acceptance criterion 3 from the issue: `OrderSubmitIntent.qty` now matches what `BinanceAdapter._snap_qty_to_lot_step` would have produced — the adapter-side snap at `submit_order` becomes a no-op for command-creation paths that route through the new `quantize_for_command`

### Add

- Add `CommandQuantization` frozen dataclass to [`praxis/infrastructure/venue_adapter.py`](praxis/infrastructure/venue_adapter.py): `snapped_qty: Decimal | None` + `rejection_reason: str | None`, with the invariant that exactly one is `None` (success → `snapped_qty` populated; rejection → `rejection_reason` carries a structured `INTAKE_*` string). Used as the return type of the new protocol method `VenueAdapter.quantize_for_command`
- Add `quantize_for_command(symbol, qty, order_type, *, reference_price=None)` to the [`VenueAdapter`](praxis/infrastructure/venue_adapter.py) protocol. Implementations floor-snap `qty` to the symbol's `LOT_SIZE.stepSize` via `(qty // step) * step` (NOT `Decimal.quantize`, which rounds to the step's decimal-exponent rather than to an integer multiple — wrong for non-pure-`10^-n` steps like `5` or `0.00001000` with eight fractional digits), gate against `LOT_SIZE.minQty` pre-snap AND post-snap (a sub-step input can snap to `0`), and gate against `NOTIONAL.minNotional` when `reference_price` is supplied. Implementations return the input qty unchanged when the symbol's filters are not yet cached — the existing `submit_order`-time snap remains the backstop and the launcher's command flow stays operational even before `exchangeInfo` lands
- Add `quantize_for_command` implementation to [`BinanceAdapter`](praxis/infrastructure/binance_adapter.py) reading from the existing `self._filters[symbol]` cache populated by `load_filters` / `get_exchange_info`. Rejection reasons follow the `INTAKE_<CHECK> <key>=<value>` convention: `INTAKE_BELOW_MIN_QTY qty=... symbol=... lot_min=...` (pre-snap minQty miss), `INTAKE_BELOW_MIN_QTY qty=... snapped=... symbol=... lot_min=... lot_step=...` (snap-to-zero case), and `INTAKE_BELOW_MIN_NOTIONAL notional=... qty=... reference_price=... symbol=... min_notional=...` (snapped notional below the venue floor). Suitable for direct emission as a Nexus `ACTION_REJECTED` reason
- Add `venue_adapter: VenueAdapter | None = None` keyword parameter to [`_build_validation_context`](praxis/launcher.py), [`_build_enter_context`](praxis/launcher.py), and [`_build_exit_context`](praxis/launcher.py). When present, the launcher routes `action.size` through `venue_adapter.quantize_for_command(symbol, action.size, quantize_order_type, reference_price=...)` BEFORE computing `order_notional`, so `ValidationRequestContext.order_size` and `ValidationRequestContext.order_notional` reflect the snapped qty all the way to capital reservation. `quantize_order_type` is derived as `translate_order_type(action.order_type) if action.order_type is not None else OrderType.MARKET` so future LIMIT / STOP actions are quantized under the venue's per-type filter rules (round-4 review fix). On rejection (`CommandQuantization.rejection_reason is not None`), the helper logs a structured WARNING with `strategy_id`, `command_id` (or `trade_id` for EXIT), `symbol`, `reference_price` / `entry_price`, `requested_size`, and the structured `reason`, then returns `None` — the caller drops the action. Wired through the `build_context` closure in [`Launcher.start`](praxis/launcher.py) to `self._trading.venue_adapter` (the canonical adapter `Trading.__init__` instantiates — either the injected one or the internally-constructed `BinanceAdapter`; round-6 critical-correctness review fix means quantization runs in the default prod wiring rather than only on explicit-injection test paths)
- Add 9 new tests in [`TestQuantizeForCommand`](tests/test_binance_adapter.py) covering: unchanged passthrough when filters are not cached, high-precision input snapped to `0.00024` against a 5-decimal lot_step, already-aligned qty preserved, `INTAKE_BELOW_MIN_QTY` rejection pre-snap, snap-to-zero on a `lot_step=5` filter with `qty=4`, `INTAKE_BELOW_MIN_NOTIONAL` rejection when snapped notional falls below the venue floor, `reference_price=None` skipping the notional gate, return-type pinning to `CommandQuantization`, and the parity invariant (re-running `_snap_qty_to_lot_step` on an already-quantized qty is a no-op — acceptance criterion 3)
- Add [`tests/test_launcher_qty_snap.py`](tests/test_launcher_qty_snap.py) (14 cases): `_build_enter_context` and `_build_exit_context` with `venue_adapter=None` preserve `action.size` unchanged (pre-fix parity); with a real `BinanceAdapter` they snap a high-precision `0.0002455253013823074467823909254` input to `0.00024`, recompute `order_notional = snapped_qty * reference_price`, and return `None` on `INTAKE_BELOW_MIN_QTY` / `INTAKE_BELOW_MIN_NOTIONAL` rejections (acceptance criterion 1 + 4). Includes a parametrized round-trip for four non-aligned `action.size` shapes and a `MagicMock`-based test that pins the `quantize_for_command(..., reference_price=...)` keyword-call contract

### Update

- Bump `vaquum-nexus` git+https pin from `bd61a0a60eefe8c55ef43719c72081193f66e097` (Nexus v0.54.0 — SnapshotScheduler + MtmLoop) to `ac0aadd6e76c593592a14e76f7be5be50340386d` (Nexus v0.55.0 — terminal-fill residual release, [Vaquum/Nexus#79](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/pull/79)). Required for the qty-side fix to land its full impact: a Praxis-side step-snap that brings `cmd.qty` onto the LOT_SIZE grid eliminates the most common cause of `fill_notional < reservation.notional` (sub-step underfills), but a venue VWAP below the reservation reference price can still leave a sub-notional residual on the entry — Nexus v0.55.0's `CapitalController.order_fill(terminal=True)` now releases that residual back to `available` / `per_strategy_deployed` instead of parking it forever. The two fixes together close both halves of the `per_strategy_deployed` ghost-residual class
- Update [`tests/test_venue_adapter.py`](tests/test_venue_adapter.py) `TestVenueAdapterProtocol.test_conforming_class_isinstance`: the `_FakeAdapter` stub adds a no-op `quantize_for_command` method so the `@runtime_checkable Protocol` `isinstance` check stays green after the new method joined the protocol surface
- Bump `version` from `0.71.0` to `0.72.0` in [`pyproject.toml`](pyproject.toml). Full Praxis test suite passes (**1450 tests, 1 skipped, 0 failures** before the Greybeard fixes below, **1457, 1 skipped** after); `ruff check praxis tests` clean
- Pre-PR Greybeard review (3 VALID_FIX, 1 VALID_TD recorded): (1) add `qty.is_finite()` + `reference_price.is_finite()` guards at the top of [`BinanceAdapter.quantize_for_command`](praxis/infrastructure/binance_adapter.py) with new `INTAKE_NON_FINITE_QTY` / `INTAKE_NON_FINITE_REFERENCE_PRICE` rejection reasons; a `Decimal('NaN')` or `Decimal('Infinity')` from any caller (replay, backtest, future custom strategy) now produces a clean structured rejection rather than `decimal.InvalidOperation` propagating uncaught into the launcher dispatch loop. (2) enforce the [`CommandQuantization`](praxis/infrastructure/venue_adapter.py) "exactly one of `snapped_qty`, `rejection_reason` non-None" invariant in `__post_init__` with `ValueError` on either-both-None or either-both-non-None — the docstring already documented the contract but a stray constructor at the call site would have silently produced a downstream contradiction. (3) remove the unreachable `snapped <= 0` half of the post-snap rejection in [`quantize_for_command`](praxis/infrastructure/binance_adapter.py); the pre-snap `qty < lot_min` rejection at the top already prevents reaching post-snap with `snapped <= 0` given `lot_min > 0`, so the disjunction obscured which branch fires the rejection. Add 4 new tests in [`TestQuantizeForCommand`](tests/test_binance_adapter.py) (NaN qty, Infinity qty, NaN reference_price, finite-check ordering before filter-cache lookup) and 4 new tests in new [`TestCommandQuantizationInvariant`](tests/test_binance_adapter.py) class (accepts snapped-only, accepts rejection-only, rejects both-None, rejects both-non-None)
- Pre-PR Greybeard review (deferred to TD): record TD-077 in [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md). On cold start, `BinanceAdapter._filters` is empty until the first `get_exchange_info` round-trip lands; `quantize_for_command(symbol, qty, ...)` returns the input `qty` unchanged in that window, so the launcher reserves capital against the un-snapped qty before `submit_order`'s `_snap_qty_to_lot_step` snaps the actual venue request down. Bounded by one `lot_step × reference_price` (e.g. `$0.80` on a BTCUSDT order at `$80k`), transient (gone after the first round-trip closes via Nexus v0.55.0's terminal-release path), but documented as a known cold-start quirk. Migration: either reject as `INTAKE_FILTERS_NOT_CACHED` (conservative, eats initial throughput), or gate `Launcher.start_dispatch` on a `get_exchange_info` await (cleaner, +100-200ms cold start)
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#132](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/132) round-1 review by zero-bang, 1 thread): fix `test_rejects_when_snap_collapses_to_zero` (renamed to `test_rejects_when_snap_collapses_below_min_qty`) in [`tests/test_binance_adapter.py`](tests/test_binance_adapter.py) so it actually exercises the post-snap `INTAKE_BELOW_MIN_QTY` branch in [`BinanceAdapter.quantize_for_command`](praxis/infrastructure/binance_adapter.py). Pre-fix the test used `lot_min=5, lot_step=5, qty=4` — but `qty=4 < lot_min=5` means the **pre-snap** guard fires and the post-snap branch (line 1173, `if snapped < filters.lot_min`) is never reached; the assertion only checked the shared `INTAKE_BELOW_MIN_QTY` substring, so the test passed for the wrong reason and provided no coverage of the post-snap re-check. Filters now `lot_step=5, lot_min=1`: `qty=4 >= 1` passes pre-snap, then `snapped = (4 // 5) * 5 = 0 < 1` correctly trips the post-snap branch. New assertions check `'snapped=0'` and `'lot_step=5'` substrings — both uniquely produced by the post-snap rejection (the pre-snap variant emits neither). Also add a comment in `quantize_for_command` noting the post-snap re-check is defensive-only on real Binance Spot filters (where `minQty >= stepSize` always holds), but reachable for custom `SymbolFilters` with `lot_step > lot_min`
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#132](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/132) round-2 review by Copilot, 1 thread): change `assert result.snapped_qty is raw` to `assert result.snapped_qty == raw` in [`test_returns_unchanged_when_filters_not_cached`](tests/test_binance_adapter.py). The `quantize_for_command` contract only requires returning a numerically equal Decimal, not the same Python object — `is` over-constrains the implementation against a future refactor that copies, normalizes, or caches Decimals. Sweep audit: this was the only object-identity assertion on a Decimal value across all 31 new test cases (`TestQuantizeForCommand` + `TestCommandQuantizationInvariant` + `test_launcher_qty_snap`); every other `is` check is `is None` / `is not None`, which is correct because `None` is a singleton
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#132](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/132) round-3 review by Copilot, 1 thread): change the `_FakeAdapter.quantize_for_command` stub in [`tests/test_venue_adapter.py`](tests/test_venue_adapter.py) from `-> None: ...` to `-> CommandQuantization: return CommandQuantization(snapped_qty=Decimal('1'), rejection_reason=None)`. `isinstance(x, VenueAdapter)` only checks method names (Protocol runtime check), so the wrong return type didn't fail the test, but the stub mis-documented the API and would have returned `None` at any hypothetical call site that depended on the protocol's actual contract. Sweep audit: the other 16 `_FakeAdapter` stubs also return `-> None: ...` despite their protocol return types being non-`None` (`SubmitResult`, `CancelResult`, `VenueOrder`, etc.); those pre-date this PR's diff and Copilot didn't flag them — they remain a pre-existing cleanup opportunity not in scope here
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#132](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/132) round-4 review by Copilot, 3 threads): (1 + 2) thread `action.order_type` through to `venue_adapter.quantize_for_command` in both [`_build_enter_context`](praxis/launcher.py) and [`_build_exit_context`](praxis/launcher.py) instead of hardcoding `OrderType.MARKET`. The Nexus `Action.order_type` is the Nexus `OrderType` enum; the launcher now translates it to the Praxis `OrderType` via the existing `translate_order_type` helper, falling back to `OrderType.MARKET` only when `action.order_type` is `None` (which Nexus's `Action.__post_init__` permits for EXIT but not ENTER). Forward-compat for future LIMIT / STOP order paths so the venue's per-type filter behavior (e.g. NOTIONAL's `applyMinToMarket=true` exemption for non-MARKET) can be expressed in `quantize_for_command` without revisiting the call sites. Add 2 new tests in [`TestQuantizationStubInteraction`](tests/test_launcher_qty_snap.py): `test_threads_action_order_type_through_to_adapter` (Nexus `OrderType.LIMIT` action surfaces as Praxis `OrderType.LIMIT` in the adapter call), `test_defaults_order_type_to_market_when_exit_action_order_type_is_none` (EXIT action with `order_type=None` falls back to MARKET); (3) fix the TD-077 relative-link prefix in [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md) — was `[praxis/launcher.py](praxis/launcher.py)` (resolves to `docs/praxis/...` on GitHub, broken), now `[praxis/launcher.py](../praxis/launcher.py)` (matches the working `../tests/...` pattern used by TD-076). Same fix applied to the `binance_adapter.py` link in the same `**Module**:` line. Sweep audit: many other TD entries in the file use the prefix-less `[praxis/...](praxis/...)` form; those pre-date this PR's diff and weren't flagged in prior reviews — they remain a pre-existing cleanup opportunity not in scope here
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#132](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/132) round-6 review by Copilot, 1 thread — **critical correctness**): the `build_context` closure in [`Launcher.start`](praxis/launcher.py) was passing `self._venue_adapter` (the constructor arg, defaults to `None` in the default `main()` wiring) where it should have passed `self._trading.venue_adapter` (the canonical adapter that `Trading.__init__` always instantiates — either the injected one or an internally-constructed `BinanceAdapter`). Net effect of the bug: in default prod wiring (`Launcher(...)` called without `venue_adapter=`), `_build_*_context` saw `venue_adapter=None`, skipped the `quantize_for_command` block entirely, and the entire qty pre-snap fix this PR introduces was effectively dead code in production — `cmd.qty` would still arrive at the validator un-snapped, the `BinanceAdapter._snap_qty_to_lot_step` at `submit_order` time would do the only snap, and the `PARTIAL`-on-stepSize-rounding pattern that motivated this PR would persist. Fix: replace `venue_adapter=self._venue_adapter` with `venue_adapter=(self._trading.venue_adapter if self._trading is not None else None)`. Existing test fixtures (`MockVenueAdapter` injected via the `venue_adapter=` kwarg) would not have caught this since the injected path passes `self._venue_adapter` through identically; only the default-wiring path was broken
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#132](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/132) round-7 review by Copilot, 2 threads — both VALID_DOC): (1) update [`_build_validation_context`](praxis/launcher.py)'s docstring (line 503-onwards) to describe the `venue_adapter`-aware `order_notional` shape — was `order_notional = action.size * reference_price` / `... * action.size` (pre-fix prose), now correctly says `snapped_qty * reference_price` when `venue_adapter` is supplied and the original `action.size * reference_price` only when `venue_adapter` is `None` (e.g. in test paths). Both ENTER and EXIT bullets updated. (2) update the `### Add` bullet for the `venue_adapter` kwarg above (line 904) to describe the shipped wiring — was claiming hardcoded `OrderType.MARKET` and `self._venue_adapter`, now correctly describes the `translate_order_type(action.order_type)` derivation with `None`-fallback (round-4 fix) and the `self._trading.venue_adapter` source (round-6 critical-correctness fix). Both fixes close the documentation drift introduced by rounds 4 + 6
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#132](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/132) round-8 review by Copilot, 1 thread): mint `command_id` at the top of [`_build_enter_context`](praxis/launcher.py) via `action.command_id or f'cmd-{uuid.uuid4().hex}'` and use the minted value in all 3 rejection-path log lines (no-reference-price, no-size, `quantize_for_command` rejection). Pre-fix the rejection logs used `action.command_id` directly, which is `None` until `_build_enter_context`'s accept-path mint at the bottom of the function — so a rejected ENTER would log `command_id: null` and the operator had no correlation handle for the dropped action. Sweep audit found a same-class variant in [`_build_validation_context`](praxis/launcher.py)'s MODIFY skip log (line 576), fixed with the same `or f'cmd-{uuid.uuid4().hex}'` pattern. EXIT's rejection logs already used `trade_id` (required non-`None` at the function's earlier guard) so they're correlation-safe and untouched here
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#132](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/132) round-9 review by Copilot, 2 threads): remove the unused `_TS` module-level constant (line 50) and the now-orphaned `from datetime import UTC, datetime` import (line 17) from [`tests/test_launcher_qty_snap.py`](tests/test_launcher_qty_snap.py). Pure dead-code cleanup — ruff didn't flag them (module-level constants aren't covered by `F841`'s function-local scope) but the constant was an artifact from an earlier draft of the test file that didn't survive. Sweep audit: `UTC` / `datetime` had no other use in the file (`_BTCUSDT_FILTERS` and the action factories are the only other module-level names and they don't need either)
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#132](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/132) round-10 review by Copilot, 1 thread): move `from praxis.core.domain.enums import OrderType` from line 34 (mid-`nexus.*` block) to the `praxis.*` import section just after `from praxis.command_translator import (...)`. Pre-fix the first-party import was inserted in the middle of the third-party `nexus.*` block, breaking the file's stdlib → third-party → first-party grouping. Sweep audit: only this one import was misplaced — all other `praxis.*` imports in this file are correctly in the first-party block at line 84+. No other PR-introduced imports cross the boundary
- Post-PR-review fix (PR [#132](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/pull/132) round-11 review by Copilot, 1 thread): make `test_below_min_qty_returns_none_with_intake_rejection_logged` actually assert on the log line. Pre-fix the test claimed via its name that an intake-rejection WARNING was logged but only checked `ctx is None`; a regression that removed or silenced the warning would have passed. Test now wraps the call in `caplog.at_level('WARNING', logger='praxis.launcher')`, filters records for the exact rejection message, and asserts on the structured extras (`strategy_id == 'strat-a'`, `symbol == 'BTCUSDT'`, `requested_size == '0.000001'`, `reason` contains `INTAKE_BELOW_MIN_QTY`, `command_id` is non-`None` and starts with `cmd-` or matches `action.command_id`). The `command_id` assertion also implicitly pins the round-8 minting fix — a regression that reverts to logging raw `action.command_id` (which defaults to `None` for synthesized actions) would now fail this test. Sweep audit: this is the only test in `test_launcher_qty_snap.py` whose name implies log-assertion semantics

## v0.73.0 on 7th of June, 2026

### NOTE

- Quote-native MARKET BUY ships paired with Nexus v0.56.0 (companion PR [Vaquum/Nexus#80](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/pull/80), merged at commit `4530720887d`). Replaces the v0.72.0 200-bps reservation buffer (`order_notional = order_size * reference_price * Decimal('1.02')`) with Binance's native `quoteOrderQty` parameter on MARKET BUY ENTERs. Strategy emits `quote_qty=budget`; venue computes the executed base qty from live liquidity; reservation matches spend exactly. Eliminates the kline-staleness slippage gap at the source instead of papering over it. Prod-observed evidence (v0.72.0 monkeypatch, Jun 2026): 1658 MARKET BUY ENTERs failed `order_fill` with `fill_notional > remaining`; bps-excess distribution p10=2.4 / p50=15.7 / p90=48.7 / p99=154 / max=170 over a 15-min stale reference window. WS-path terminal handling for quote-native is recorded as TD-079 against this PR and tracked as a follow-up

### Add

- Bump `vaquum-nexus` pin from `ac0aadd6` (v0.55.0) to `4530720887d` (PR #80 merge — quote-native MARKET BUY plumbing). Imports `Action.quote_qty`, `TradeCommand.quote_qty`, and the `PraxisOutbound.send_command(quote_qty=...)` conditional kwarg so Praxis's `Trading.submit_command` receives `quote_qty=` from Nexus on quote-native ENTERs
- Add `quote_qty: Decimal | None = None` field to [`TradeCommand`](praxis/core/domain/trade_command.py) with `is_quote_native` property; `__post_init__` requires exactly one of `qty` or `quote_qty` to be set; both positive-Decimal validated. The property is the canonical read site for any code path that needs to branch behavior on the order shape
- Add `quote_qty: Decimal | None = None` field to [`Order`](praxis/core/domain/order.py); `qty` made `Decimal | None`; `__post_init__` enforces the same XOR contract; `remaining_qty` returns `None` for quote-native (no base target known). `_update_order_on_fill` in [`trading_state.py`](praxis/core/trading_state.py) stops at `PARTIALLY_FILLED` for quote-native (no implicit FILLED transition); the explicit `OrderQuoteNativeFilled` event flips it
- Add `quote_qty: Decimal | None = None` field to [`OrderSubmitIntent`](praxis/core/domain/events.py) with the same XOR enforcement; threaded through the spine so [`event_spine`](praxis/infrastructure/event_spine.py) replay reconstructs `TradeCommand.quote_qty` correctly
- Add `OrderQuoteNativeFilled` spine event to [`events.py`](praxis/core/domain/events.py) and to the `Event` union; registered in [`event_spine._EVENT_REGISTRY`](praxis/infrastructure/event_spine.py). Carries only `client_order_id` + `timestamp`. `TradingState._on_order_quote_native_filled` flips the order's status to `OrderStatus.FILLED` and calls `_close_order` — the terminal signal for quote-native orders since `filled_qty >= qty` is undefined when `qty is None`
- Add `quote_qty: Decimal | None = None` kwarg to the [`VenueAdapter.submit_order`](praxis/infrastructure/venue_adapter.py) protocol and to [`BinanceAdapter.submit_order`](praxis/infrastructure/binance_adapter.py). When `quote_qty` is set, dispatches via a new `_build_quote_native_market_params` helper that sends Binance's `quoteOrderQty` parameter; rejects non-MARKET / non-BUY / dual-set combinations with `ValueError` (caught upstream by `ExecutionManager._process_command` and routed to `_record_submit_failed`)
- Add `quoteOrderQty` support to [`binsim.server`](praxis/binsim/server.py): the POST `/api/v3/order` endpoint accepts `quoteOrderQty` for MARKET BUY (mutually exclusive with `quantity`); enforces the BUY-only constraint to match Binance; rejects `quoteOrderQty` with non-positive Decimal. [`binsim.book.consume_quote_for_market_buy`](praxis/binsim/book.py) walks the ask ladder until the quote budget is exhausted (partial-takes the last level to land exactly on `quote_qty`)
- Add TD-078 to [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md): `estimate_slippage` is observation-only; no execution guard rejects bad-fill MARKET orders. Deferred from the quote-native scope
- Add TD-079 to [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md): quote-native terminal signal arrives only on the REST submit-response path; WS-driven fills set the order to `PARTIALLY_FILLED` and the WS reconcile path does not emit `OrderQuoteNativeFilled`. The current trading shape is REST-synchronous so this is latent; tracked as a follow-up before any LIMIT or post-only quote-native path lands

### Update

- Branch [`_build_enter_context`](praxis/launcher.py) on `action.quote_qty`: when set, bypasses the `quantize_for_command` step (LOT_SIZE snapping is venue-side for quote-native), uses `action.quote_qty` as `order_notional` directly, leaves `order_size=None`. The qty-native path keeps the v0.72.0 pre-snap (`snapped_qty * reference_price`) and drops the v0.72.0 200-bps multiplier (now `order_notional = order_size * reference_price` exact). `_ensure_entry_position` falls back to a `Decimal('1')` sentinel for entry_price when `action.quote_qty is not None` and no reference_price is available — the placeholder's entry_price is overwritten on the first fill via `_grow_position`'s VWAP when `old_size == 0`
- Make [`TradingState.mark_quote_native_filled`](praxis/core/trading_state.py) (later replaced by the `OrderQuoteNativeFilled` event handler) gate the terminal flip on `filled_qty > _ZERO`. Closes the rescue zero-fill regression: when the rescue path returned `status=FILLED` with empty `immediate_fills` it had been emitting a zero-fill outcome that suppressed the later WS fills. Status-branch in [`ExecutionManager._process_command`](praxis/core/execution_manager.py) similarly requires `result.status == FILLED AND filled_qty > _ZERO` before emitting `TradeStatus.FILLED`
- Catch adapter `ValueError` in [`ExecutionManager._process_command`](praxis/core/execution_manager.py) submit try-block and route to `_record_submit_failed` (rather than letting it propagate as an unhandled exception that parked capital)
- Branch [`validate_trade_command._validate_venue_filters`](praxis/core/validate_trade_command.py) on `cmd.is_quote_native`: skip LOT_SIZE checks (venue applies them at fill time), validate `cmd.quote_qty >= filters.min_notional` instead. Adds `_validate_quote_native_shape` to reject quote_qty with non-BUY or non-MARKET at intake time
- Branch [`ExecutionManager._process_command`](praxis/core/execution_manager.py) on `cmd.is_quote_native`: skip `estimate_slippage` (observation-only and based on base qty), skip the immediate-fills overfill clamp, skip the WS-driven `emitted_filled_qty > cmd.qty` clamp. Thread `quote_qty=cmd.quote_qty` to the `OrderSubmitIntent` and to `submit_order`. The replay path at line 369 also threads `quote_qty=event.quote_qty` when reconstructing `TradeCommand` from the spine
- Make [`TradeOutcome.target_qty`](praxis/core/domain/trade_outcome.py) `Decimal | None` (quote-native has no base target). `fill_ratio` returns `None` when `target_qty is None`. [`OutcomeTranslator._build_partial`](praxis/outcome_translator.py) and `_build_terminal` compute `remaining_size = target_qty - filled_qty if target_qty is not None else None`

### Fix

- Validator-pipeline rejection of quote_qty on non-BUY / non-MARKET shapes now fires at intake, before any reservation is taken — symmetric with Nexus's `Action.__post_init__` guard
- Quote-native terminal Order state is now persisted via the new `OrderQuoteNativeFilled` event; spine replay reconstructs the closed state instead of leaving the order stranded at `PARTIALLY_FILLED`
- `_ensure_entry_position` no longer requires a reference_price for quote-native ENTERs (placeholder uses `Decimal('1')` sentinel; first fill overwrites). The strategy's `_reference_price` helper now sanitizes signal-derived Decimals to `None` on non-finite or non-positive, but that lives on the Nexus side (companion PR)

## v0.74.0 on 8th of June, 2026

### NOTE

- `OrderRejectedError` no longer counts toward the venue HealthTracker's `consecutive_failures` / `failure_rate`. The venue signaling `-2010 INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE` / `-1013 MIN_NOTIONAL` / `-1013 LOT_SIZE` / `-1100 ILLEGAL_PARAMETER` (and any other business-rule rejection code) means the venue is healthy and refused the specific order's parameters or the account's current balance state. The HealthLoop should react to venue-availability problems (timeouts, 5xx, auth, rate limits), not to per-order business-rule outcomes. Same class of design defect as the `NotFoundError`-when-binsim exemption that shipped in #114; the class sweep then should have included `OrderRejectedError` too. Prod-observed motivation: a v0.73.0 deploy halted after two consecutive ENTERs with stale binsim ledger balance (`-2010` from binsim) tripped the consecutive-failure threshold, flipped `state.mode` to HALTED, every subsequent ENTER got rejected with `INTAKE_MODE_BLOCKS_ENTER`, and no fresh signed successes could dilute the failure window — permanent halt despite later SELLs that would have refilled USDT had they been allowed to run.

### Fix

- Insert `except OrderRejectedError:` clause in [`BinanceAdapter._request_with_retry`](praxis/infrastructure/binance_adapter.py) before the generic `except VenueError` handler. The new clause calls `_record_health(account_id, start, succeeded=True)` before re-raising — the venue responded successfully and applied its business rules correctly, so the round-trip is a positive health signal: the latency sample is real and `_consecutive_failures` resets, preventing a prior transient failure from persisting through a chain of business-rule rejections. `AuthenticationError`, `RateLimitError`, `TransientError` (including transport-layer wrap from `aiohttp.ClientError` / `TimeoutError`), `NotFoundError` on real venues (the existing #114 binsim gate is untouched), and `OrderSubmitTimeoutError` continue to be recorded as health failures. `DuplicateClientOrderIdError` is constructed downstream by [`submit_order`](praxis/infrastructure/binance_adapter.py) when the underlying `OrderRejectedError`'s `venue_code` matches `_DUPLICATE_CLIENT_ORDER_ID_CODE` (note: `-2010` is overloaded in Binance for both INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE and DUPLICATE_CLIENT_ORDER_ID, so the prod path always sees the rejection upward as `DuplicateClientOrderIdError`); the record-as-success here applies regardless of how the caller classifies the raise. Replaces `tests/test_binance_adapter.py::test_non_not_found_venue_error_still_records_with_binsim_url` with four focused tests: `test_order_rejected_error_records_as_success_and_resets_consecutive_failures` (`-2010` INSUFFICIENT_BALANCE — seeds a prior failure, asserts the rejection resets `consecutive_failures` to 0 and contributes a positive latency sample), `test_min_notional_rejection_records_as_success_and_resets_consecutive_failures` (`-1013` MIN_NOTIONAL — same shape), `test_authentication_error_still_records_health_failure` (HTTP 401 — scope-pin proving the exemption is bounded to business-rule classes), and `test_submit_order_with_client_order_id_records_success_on_2010` (end-to-end through `submit_order` with a `client_order_id` — asserts `DuplicateClientOrderIdError` is raised AND `consecutive_failures` resets, anchoring the prod-path claim that the underlying exemption fires regardless of the downstream wrap)

## v0.75.0 on 9th of June, 2026

### NOTE

- [Vaquum/Praxis#135](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/135): binsim `Ledger._snapshot_locked` serialized the full ledger — every account, the full `fills` history, the full `seen_client_order_ids` set — to JSON and wrote it synchronously on the asyncio event loop, inside the `asyncio.Lock` held across settlement, on every `apply_order` / `apply_fill`. `Account.fills` was unbounded so payload size grew linearly with cumulative order count; per-order latency was O(n) and cumulative cost O(n²). Measured: 50 k fills → 117 ms per write, 40× throughput collapse (315 → 7.8 ord/s), p99 latency 94 ms → 4.1 s, and event-loop jitter up to 4.3 s — past the 5 s `BINSIM_STALENESS_MS` default, so the depth poller starves and `-1003` rejections cascade. At observed prod fill rates the 50 k threshold lands inside three days. This release reduces the field-of-attack at the source: `fills[]` is dropped from snapshot persistence (no production reader — `binsim.server._my_trades_stub` returns `[]`), and the remaining snapshot write moves off the event loop via `asyncio.to_thread`. **Residual growth not addressed by this PR**: `Account.seen_client_order_ids` is still persisted via `_account_to_dict` (called inside `_snapshot_locked` on the event loop, including a `sorted(...)` on the full set per snapshot), so the snapshot payload still grows linearly with cumulative order count via that field — and the `sorted` call remains on the loop above the `to_thread` boundary. The `apply_order` hot path adds one entry to the set per order; `apply_fill` does not. Dominated-term-only mitigation, not full constant-sizing. The in-memory `Account.fills` list and `Account.seen_client_order_ids` set also remain unbounded; recorded against the same module as TD-081 so the next bound pass closes both the persistence and on-loop-sort components.

### Update

- Drop `fills` from [`_account_to_dict`](praxis/binsim/ledger.py) and stop persisting it. `Ledger.fills(account_id)` still returns the in-memory list for tests and any future in-process consumer; the data is now process-lifetime rather than restart-durable. `binsim.server._my_trades_stub` already returns `[]` so no production endpoint observed the persisted fills — the change is invisible to clients. [`_account_from_dict`](praxis/binsim/ledger.py) tolerates legacy snapshots that still carry `fills` (silently ignored) and initializes `Account.fills=[]` on every load
- Convert [`Ledger._snapshot_locked`](praxis/binsim/ledger.py) to async and route the disk I/O through `await asyncio.to_thread(self._write_snapshot_atomic, payload)`. The lock stays held across the await (so concurrent mutations remain serialised) but the event loop is freed for the depth poller and other request handlers during the write. Updates the three call sites in [`register_account`](praxis/binsim/ledger.py), [`apply_fill`](praxis/binsim/ledger.py), and [`apply_order`](praxis/binsim/ledger.py) to `await self._snapshot_locked()`. The atomic write itself (`mkstemp` → `write_text` → `replace`) lives in a new sync helper `_write_snapshot_atomic` so the `asyncio.to_thread` boundary is a single function call. Removes now-dead `_fill_to_dict` / `_fill_from_dict` helpers
- Update [`tests/test_binsim_ledger.py`](tests/test_binsim_ledger.py): rename `test_load_restores_balances_and_fills` → `test_load_restores_balances_and_counters` (asserts balances + `next_trade_id` survive load, `fills` does not). Rename `test_ledger_fill_record_round_trips_through_snapshot` → `test_fills_do_not_survive_snapshot_round_trip` (asserts the persisted shape no longer carries fills). `test_snapshot_written_after_register_and_fill` swaps `len(payload[...]['fills']) == 1` for `'fills' not in payload[...]`. Adds four regression tests: `test_snapshot_size_stays_bounded_under_fill_growth_only` (after 100 `apply_fill` calls the snapshot file is within 256 B of the empty baseline — the `fills`-term pathology), `test_apply_order_still_grows_snapshot_via_seen_client_order_ids` (after 100 `apply_order` calls with distinct `client_order_id`s the snapshot DID grow past a floor — documents the residual growth as fact, not a regression, so a future reader doesn't read the bounded-under-fill test as proving the whole snapshot is bounded), `test_legacy_snapshot_with_fills_field_loads_balances` (a snapshot from before this PR still loads, balances restore, the persisted-fills field is silently dropped on the next snapshot write), and `test_snapshot_write_completes_when_caller_cancelled` (instruments `_write_snapshot_atomic` with a synthetic delay, cancels the `apply_fill` task mid-write, asserts the in-flight write completes before the lock is released — covers the cancellation race the `asyncio.shield` + `finally` pattern in `_snapshot_locked` exists to prevent)

### Fix

- Closes [Vaquum/Praxis#135](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/135). Bounds the per-order snapshot cost to a constant (proportional to account count, not cumulative fill count) and unblocks the event loop during the write — the two conditions Mikko's measurements identified as the cause of the 40× throughput collapse and the depth-poller-staleness cascade

## v0.76.0 on 9th of June, 2026

### NOTE

- [Vaquum/Praxis#142](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/142): in prod (epoch=16, v0.75.0, ~3h post-deploy on 2026-06-09) 1,325 trades were stuck in an infinite EXIT-retry loop, each emitting an `INTAKE_BELOW_MIN_QTY` rejection at [`praxis/launcher.py:_build_exit_context`](praxis/launcher.py) every 15–30 min. Two blocker paths flow through the same function — post-fill sub-lot residue (B1, fixed Nexus-side in [Vaquum/Nexus#82](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/issues/82) at `OutcomeProcessor._reduce_position`) and pre-submit sub-lot rejection (B2, fixed here in `_build_exit_context`'s rejection branch). The Nexus companion (v0.57.0) introduced `intended_full_close` on both `ValidationRequestContext` and `OrderContext`, plus `OutcomeProcessor.close_as_dust(trade_id, reason, dust_close_id)` + a durable `InstanceState.account_dust: dict[str, Decimal]` ledger. This Praxis release computes the intent flag at the launcher boundary, threads it through both context classes (required because `_build_order_context` re-runs `build_context` rather than passing the original through), and calls `close_as_dust` when intake-time venue quantization rejects a full-close EXIT as sub-lot. After Praxis v0.76.0 + Nexus v0.57.0 land together, the cycle Mikko measured at the 1,325-stuck-trades threshold is closed at both entry points.

### Update

- Bump [`vaquum-nexus`](pyproject.toml) git-pin to `7ce0260` (Nexus v0.57.0). The new pin makes `ValidationRequestContext.intended_full_close`, `OrderContext.intended_full_close`, `InstanceState.account_dust`, and `OutcomeProcessor.close_as_dust` reachable from Praxis. The pin update is paired with the launcher changes below — earlier Praxis revisions cannot reference `close_as_dust` because Nexus v0.56.0 doesn't expose it
- Update [`praxis/launcher.py:_build_exit_context`](praxis/launcher.py) to compute `remaining = position.size - position.pending_exit` and `intended_full_close = action.size == remaining` (exact `Decimal` equality), then set `intended_full_close` on the returned `ValidationRequestContext`. The `command_id` derivation moves above the quantization step so a stable command id is in scope across the rejection branch's `extra` dict and downstream
- Update [`praxis/launcher.py:_build_exit_context`](praxis/launcher.py)'s rejection branch (previously `return None` after logging `'EXIT action rejected by venue filters at intake; skipping'`): when `intended_full_close=True` AND the caller supplied an `outcome_processor`, call `OutcomeProcessor.close_as_dust(trade_id=trade_id, reason=quantization.rejection_reason, dust_close_id=f'dust-{trade_id}')` before returning. The dedup key is `trade_id`-derived (not `command_id`-derived) so it stays deterministic even when `action.command_id is None` and the launcher generates a fresh per-call UUID — a position can only become dust once per epoch, so `trade_id` is the natural key for that invariant. Partial-close rejections still drop silently — the close_as_dust path only fires for full-position EXIT intent. The rejection log now includes `intended_full_close` AND `command_id` so the operator can correlate the rejection with a specific strategy action at a glance
- Update [`praxis/launcher.py:_build_order_context`](praxis/launcher.py) at the `OrderContext` construction site to copy `validation_context.intended_full_close` onto `OrderContext.intended_full_close`. Required because `_build_order_context` re-runs `build_context(action, strategy_id)` rather than passing the original `ValidationRequestContext` through, so a field only on `OrderContext` would be lost across the rebuild
- Update [`praxis/launcher.py:_build_validation_context`](praxis/launcher.py) signature to accept an optional `outcome_processor: OutcomeProcessor | None = None` kwarg and thread it into `_build_exit_context`. The caller in the runtime `build_context` closure (where `outcome_processor` is in scope after construction) passes the live instance through

### Add

- Add [`tests/test_launcher_validation_context.py::TestExitContextIntendedFullCloseFlag`](tests/test_launcher_validation_context.py) (3 cases): `intended_full_close=True` when `action.size == position.size - position.pending_exit` (the prod hot path with `pending_exit=0`); same `True` result when `pending_exit > 0` and `action.size == remaining`; `False` on a deliberate partial-exit
- Add [`tests/test_launcher_validation_context.py::TestExitContextDustCloseRouting`](tests/test_launcher_validation_context.py) (4 cases): full-close EXIT rejected by venue quantization routes to `OutcomeProcessor.close_as_dust` with `dust_close_id=f'dust-{trade_id}'` and the venue's `rejection_reason`; partial-close rejection does NOT route to `close_as_dust` (the close-as-dust path is gated on `intended_full_close=True`); full-close rejection without an `outcome_processor` falls back to the legacy `return None` behavior so test callers that don't pass one still work; two `_build_validation_context` calls with `command_id=None` on the same trade produce identical `dust_close_id` values so the Nexus-side `_processed_dust_close_ids` dedup catches the second one
- Add [`tests/test_launcher_validation_context.py::TestOrderContextCarriesIntendedFullClose`](tests/test_launcher_validation_context.py) (2 cases) — regression for the field-only-on-`OrderContext` bug codex flagged in the Nexus audit. Constructs a `_build_order_context` call with a `build_context_for_test` closure that re-runs `_build_validation_context`, verifies the flag survives the rebuild for both full-close and partial-exit shapes
- Uses minimal `_FakeVenueAdapterRejecting` (returns a rejecting `CommandQuantization`) and `_RecordingOutcomeProcessor` (captures `close_as_dust` calls into a list) stubs in the same test file rather than mocking `BinanceAdapter` — keeps the test scope tight to the launcher decision logic

### Fix

- Closes [Vaquum/Praxis#142](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/142). Companion Nexus-side fix at [Vaquum/Nexus#82](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/issues/82) (shipped as Nexus v0.57.0, merged at commit `7ce0260`). The two PRs are paired: Praxis alone cannot reach `close_as_dust` because Nexus must define it first; the matching Nexus changes (`intended_full_close` on both context classes, `account_dust` ledger on `InstanceState` with WAL/snapshot codec roundtrip, the `close_as_dust` method itself) shipped earlier today

## v0.77.0 on 10th of June, 2026

### NOTE

- During the v0.76.0 paper-trade validation (2026-06-10), positions were not closing: 175 of 190 SELL EXITs in one epoch left `state.positions` entries at full pre-fill size with `pending_exit` still set, and the new `close_as_dust` path then read that stale state and moved full positions (~0.0325 BTC each) into `account_dust` — 8.75 BTC of phantom dust in one epoch. Root cause: Nexus position/capital accounting is applied by the `OutcomeLoop`'s single worker, which serializes it with strategy callbacks and action submission; under load, translated FILLED outcomes lag behind strategy ticks, so strategies and the dust path act on stale `state.positions`. This release applies accounting synchronously at the Praxis outcome boundary and gates the dust-close on no-EXIT-in-flight, closing both halves

### Update

- Bump [`vaquum-nexus`](pyproject.toml) git-pin to Nexus v0.58.0, which makes `OutcomeProcessor._handle_ack` context-aware: EXIT ACKs are a no-op success instead of the structural `order_ack failed: order not found` failure (EXIT commands never reserve capital, so no `TrackedOrder` exists for them)
- Update [`vaquum_limen`](pyproject.toml) git-pin from the `v4.0.1` tag to the commit it resolved to (`06815fab`) — the tag no longer exists on the Limen remote and its absence breaks dependency install in CI. Installed content is identical; only the reference form changes
- Update [`praxis/launcher.py:_build_exit_context`](praxis/launcher.py)'s dust-close branch with a `position.pending_exit == _ZERO` gate: when a prior EXIT order is in flight, the rejection no longer dusts the position — the in-flight fill will reduce `position.size` when it lands and the strategy's next tick re-evaluates against the true residue. Dusting with an EXIT in flight moved the not-yet-sold quantity into `account_dust`
- Update [`praxis/launcher.py:_route_translated`](praxis/launcher.py) to apply the Nexus in-memory mutation synchronously before enqueuing each translated outcome for the async `OutcomeLoop`, via the new `Launcher._apply_sync_accounting`. Persistence stays off the Trading event loop: mutated command ids are generation-stamped into `unpersisted_commands` and the async `process_outcome` — on the `OutcomeLoop` worker thread — performs the `append_mutation` on the dedup-hit redelivery, withholds `OutcomeAcked` until it succeeds, and discards an id only when its generation still equals the pre-append snapshot's (so a command re-mutated during the serialize stays pending), preserving the boot-replay durability contract. The stale-positions race is closed by the synchronous state mutation, not by synchronous persistence

### Add

- Add [`_AccountOutcomeWiring`](praxis/launcher.py) dataclass (non-frozen, matching `_NexusRuntime` — it carries mutable members by reference): per-account references (`outcome_processor`, `command_contexts`, `command_registry_lock`, `unpersisted_commands`) registered by `_build_nexus_runtime` and consumed by `_route_translated` for the synchronous accounting path
- Add [`Launcher._apply_sync_accounting`](praxis/launcher.py) static method: looks up the outcome's `OrderContext` under the registry lock (skips when not yet registered — the async path covers those once the submitter completes registration), calls `OutcomeProcessor.process`, records mutated command ids in `unpersisted_commands` for the async consumer's persist, and contains all failures so accounting errors never block strategy-callback delivery
- Add [`tests/test_launcher_sync_accounting.py`](tests/test_launcher_sync_accounting.py) (6 cases): position mutation marks the command unpersisted; capital mutation marks the command unpersisted; skip on missing `OrderContext`; failure result does not mark; success without mutation does not mark; processor exception contained
- Add `test_full_close_rejection_with_pending_exit_does_not_route_to_close_as_dust` to [`tests/test_launcher_validation_context.py`](tests/test_launcher_validation_context.py): a sub-lot full-close rejection with `pending_exit > 0` must NOT call `close_as_dust`

## v0.78.0 on 11th of June, 2026

### NOTE

- Companion to Nexus v0.59.0's unresolved-outcome retry: the registration-gap race (translated outcomes reaching the accounting paths ~1ms before the submitter's post-`send_command` registration loop made `command_strategy_ids` / `command_contexts` visible) surfaced in epoch 24 as two venue-filled EXITs whose accounting never ran. The Nexus side now retries; this release makes the Praxis-side skip observable

### Update

- Bump [`vaquum-nexus`](pyproject.toml) git-pin to Nexus v0.59.0 (`OutcomeLoop` retries unresolved-strategy outcomes on a backoff schedule instead of dropping them)
- Update [`Launcher._apply_sync_accounting`](praxis/launcher.py): the missing-`OrderContext` skip is no longer silent — it logs a warning with `account_id`, `command_id`, `outcome_id`, `outcome_type`, and `has_strategy_mapping` (whether the strategy registry already contains the command, checked in the same lock hold as the context lookup), distinguishing the pre-registration race from a genuinely unknown command

### Add

- Add `account_id` and `command_strategy_ids` to [`_AccountOutcomeWiring`](praxis/launcher.py) — telemetry-only references for the skip warning, registered alongside the existing wiring fields

## v0.79.0 on 12th of June, 2026

### Add

- Add optional `command_id: str | None = None` to [`ExecutionManager.submit_command`](praxis/core/execution_manager.py): a caller-supplied identifier is used verbatim (treated as an opaque non-empty string; an identifier already in use by any accepted or in-memory command fails inbound validation rather than being regenerated, and the identity is reserved before the spine append's await so concurrent same-id submissions cannot interleave), and a UUID is minted exactly as before when omitted. This lets the caller know the command's identity before the handoff and register it in its own state pre-submit — the enabling half of the cross-repo fix for the registration-gap race ([#146](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/146); companion Nexus changes track [Vaquum/Nexus#86](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/issues/86) and [Vaquum/Nexus#87](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/issues/87))
- Add [`validate_command_id_for_client_order_id`](praxis/core/generate_client_order_id.py) and enforce it in `submit_command`: a caller-supplied `command_id` must have at least 16 characters after stripping hyphens (the `generate_client_order_id` derivation floor), so a too-short id is rejected at the inbound boundary before a `CommandAccepted` is persisted rather than failing later at submission. `generate_client_order_id` now delegates its inline length check to the same validator
- Add tests to [`tests/test_execution_manager.py::TestSubmitCommand`](tests/test_execution_manager.py): caller-supplied id used verbatim and registered; empty id rejected; too-short id rejected before any `CommandAccepted` reaches the spine; in-use id rejected; concurrent same-id submissions — exactly one wins and exactly one `CommandAccepted` reaches the spine; a failed/cancelled spine append frees the reservation for reuse

### Update

- Update [`praxis/launcher.py:_build_exit_context`](praxis/launcher.py): the venue-filter intake rejection logs at info when `intended_full_close` is set — that rejection IS the designed dust-close trigger (v0.76.0) and was producing 1,000+ warning lines per trading cycle for nominal behavior. Rejections of partial-close EXITs keep the warning level ([#147](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/147))
- Add `test_full_close_rejection_logs_info_partial_logs_warning` to [`tests/test_launcher_validation_context.py`](tests/test_launcher_validation_context.py) pinning the level split

## v0.80.0 on 13th of June, 2026

### NOTE

- Closes the registration-gap race at its root rather than after the fact. Earlier releases (v0.78.0) made the race *observable* and (Nexus v0.59.0) *retried* the outcomes it dropped; this release removes the gap. The launcher now registers a command's strategy mapping, capital order, position effect, and `OrderContext` BEFORE the `send_command` handoff — using the caller-supplied identity from v0.79.0 — so a fast venue's ACK/FILL resolves against state that already exists and the post-handoff registration loop is gone. Paired with Nexus v0.60.0 ([Vaquum/Nexus#86](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/issues/86), [#87](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/issues/87), [#88](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/issues/88)), which transmits the deterministic command id, treats a `send_command` timeout as `SUBMISSION_UNKNOWN` (retain the reservation+registration; the command may still execute) instead of releasing capital, and serializes the WAL/snapshot codec. This release also wires the `StateSnapshotLocks` snapshot-lock ownership move (Nexus v0.61.0 deleted the external checkpoint wraps; v0.62.0 added `StateStore.attach_snapshot_locks`, [Vaquum/Nexus#92](https://github.com/Vaquum/Nexus/issues/92)): the launcher now attaches the bundle to its `StateStore` after recovery, so the store owns the `positions_lock` + `capital_lock` serialization and the launcher drops its external `append_mutation` / scheduler / shutdown wraps

### Update

- Bump [`vaquum-nexus`](pyproject.toml) git-pin to Nexus v0.62.0 (`b59a4e7`): `submit_actions` accepts a `pre_register` callback invoked before the `send_command` handoff and returns a `SubmissionRegistration` handle (`mark_submitted` / `mark_unknown` / `rollback`); a handoff timeout resolves to `SUBMISSION_UNKNOWN` rather than `SUBMIT_FAILED`; `PraxisOutbound` exposes `supports_command_id`; `StateStore` owns the snapshot-serialization locks (`ShutdownSequencer` / `SnapshotScheduler` no longer wrap `checkpoint()`, so their lock parameters are gone) and exposes `attach_snapshot_locks` for post-recovery wiring
- Update [`praxis/launcher.py`](praxis/launcher.py) action-submission path to register before handoff: the `build_context` builder is wrapped to record each command's `(action, strategy_id, OrderContext)` by `command_id` (ids are assigned inside `build_context`, so the recording happens as they are minted), and `submit_actions` is called with that recording builder plus a `pre_register` callback (only when `praxis_outbound.supports_command_id`). The legacy post-`send_command` registration loop is skipped on the pre-registration path
- Update [`praxis/launcher.py:_build_nexus_runtime`](praxis/launcher.py) to attach the snapshot-lock bundle to its `StateStore` immediately after recovery (once `state` / `positions_lock` / `capital_controller` exist and `state.risk.lock` is wired to `positions_lock`), via `state_store.attach_snapshot_locks(_build_state_snapshot_locks(...))`, before any runtime consumer starts. The store now acquires `positions_lock` + `capital_lock` internally around `serialize_state`, so the external `with positions_lock:` wrap around the async `append_mutation` is dropped (it would re-acquire the non-reentrant lock and self-deadlock), and `SnapshotScheduler` / `ShutdownSequencer` are constructed without the now-removed lock parameters

### Add

- Add [`_PreRegisteredSubmission`](praxis/launcher.py) and the module-level [`_make_pre_register`](praxis/launcher.py) factory: `pre_register` inserts the strategy mapping, consumes the capital reservation into an order (`send_order`, raising on failure so the command is not handed off), applies the position effect (ENTER places a zero-size placeholder via `_ensure_entry_position`; EXIT increments `pending_exit`), and builds + registers the `OrderContext` from the validator's already-captured context — all before `send_command`. The `OrderContext` is built from the captured context rather than by re-running `build_context`, because the EXIT path has just incremented `pending_exit` and a re-run would recompute `intended_full_close` (`action.size == size − pending_exit`) against the inflated value, flipping a true full-close to not-full-close and breaking the dust-close path. The returned handle records exactly what it inserted so `rollback` is precise and idempotent, and `mark_unknown` retains every registration (a late outcome must resolve against it). The post-`send_order` body (position effect + context build + insert) runs under a `try` that, on any exception, undoes the position effect, recovers the orphaned capital order, and pops the registries before re-raising — so a raise after the reservation is consumed cannot leak the capital order plus strategy mapping (Nexus's `_release_granted_reservation` is a no-op for an already-consumed reservation)
- Add [`_UnknownSubmission`](praxis/launcher.py) frozen record (`command_id`, `strategy_id`, `created_at`, `action_type`, `symbol`, `side`, `order_notional`, `error`): `mark_unknown` stamps one with `datetime.now(UTC)` when a handoff times out, retaining the registration. [`process_outcome`](praxis/launcher.py) pops the record on the first successfully-processed outcome for that command (including an EXIT ACK no-op success) and defensively on terminal cleanup; a failed-processing outcome leaves it unknown
- Add [`_UnknownSubmissionMonitor`](praxis/launcher.py): a `threading.Timer` scan loop (mirroring `SnapshotScheduler`) that warns about commands stuck in `SUBMISSION_UNKNOWN` past a threshold, logging `count`, `max_age_seconds`, and a bounded `command_ids` list. Telemetry only — no venue query, no forced release. Intervals via `NEXUS_UNKNOWN_SUBMISSION_WARN_SECONDS` (default 60) and `NEXUS_UNKNOWN_SUBMISSION_SCAN_SECONDS` (default 15); started in `_build_nexus_runtime`, stopped on shutdown
- Add [`_build_state_snapshot_locks`](praxis/launcher.py): the composition-point helper that builds the `StateSnapshotLocks` bundle and asserts `state.risk.lock is positions_lock` (so the bundle's `positions_lock` covers `state.risk.per_strategy` serialization). This is the single enforcement point for that identity now that the Nexus-side construction guards are gone
- Add [`tests/test_launcher_pre_registration.py`](tests/test_launcher_pre_registration.py) (12 cases): ENTER registers before handoff; `send_order` failure raises and leaves no registration; a post-`send_order` exception (a non-positive entry price) recovers the capital order and pops the registries; the registered EXIT `OrderContext` carries the captured `intended_full_close` even after `pending_exit` is incremented; ENTER rollback removes the placeholder; EXIT rollback decrements `pending_exit`; `mark_unknown` retains the registration and records the metadata; registries are populated when `send_command` is entered (cross-module, real `submit_actions`); the monitor is silent below threshold and warns above with `count` / `max_age_seconds` / `command_ids`; `stop` cancels a pending scan; and an AST check that `process_outcome` pops the unknown only on the success branch
- Document `NEXUS_UNKNOWN_SUBMISSION_WARN_SECONDS` / `NEXUS_UNKNOWN_SUBMISSION_SCAN_SECONDS` in [`docs/Launcher.md`](docs/Launcher.md)'s Optional-environment table
- Add [`tests/test_launcher_snapshot_lock_wiring.py`](tests/test_launcher_snapshot_lock_wiring.py) (5 cases): `_build_state_snapshot_locks` returns the shared `positions_lock` + `capital_controller.lock_cm()`; it raises when `state.risk.lock` is not `positions_lock`; and AST checks that `_build_nexus_runtime` builds the bundle via the helper and calls `attach_snapshot_locks` before both `outcome_loop.start()` (the `append_mutation` path) and `snapshot_scheduler.start()` (the checkpoint path) — the attach-before-consumers ordering

## v0.80.1 on 14th of June, 2026

### NOTE

- Completes the deterministic-command-id passthrough that v0.79.0 / v0.80.0 left half-wired. Those releases taught the bottom layer ([`ExecutionManager.submit_command`](praxis/core/execution_manager.py)) to accept and honour a caller-supplied `command_id`, but the two intermediary submission seams between the launcher and the execution layer — [`Trading.submit_command`](praxis/trading.py) and [`TradingInbound.submit_command`](praxis/trading_inbound.py) — did not declare the parameter, so the deterministic id transmitted by Nexus reached a signature that rejected it and every submission raised `TypeError: submit_command() got an unexpected keyword argument 'command_id'`. The existing tests mocked submission at the `PraxisOutbound` boundary and never drove the real `Trading` → `TradingInbound` → `ExecutionManager` chain, so the gap was invisible to the suite. No capital leaked: each failed submission was rolled back cleanly by the pre-registration handle, but no order could be placed

### Fix

- Update [`praxis/trading.py:submit_command`](praxis/trading.py) and [`praxis/trading_inbound.py:submit_command`](praxis/trading_inbound.py) (both the `TradingInbound` implementation and its `_ExecutionInboundGateway` Protocol) to declare `command_id: str | None = None` and forward it to the next layer, so a Nexus-supplied deterministic id flows launcher → `Trading` → `TradingInbound` → `ExecutionManager` intact
- Update [`praxis/launcher.py:submit_command_with_translated_params`](praxis/launcher.py) to accept `command_id` as an explicit keyword parameter (rather than only through `**kwargs`) and forward it to `trading.submit_command`, so `PraxisOutbound` feature-detection classifies the id as authoritative instead of best-effort

### Add

- Add `TestSubmitCommandRealChain` to [`tests/test_execution_manager.py`](tests/test_execution_manager.py): drives a caller-supplied `command_id` through the real `TradingInbound` → `ExecutionManager` chain and asserts the returned id and the emitted `CommandAccepted.command_id` both equal the supplied id (no `TypeError`), plus a boundary case asserting a too-short id is rejected before any `CommandAccepted` is appended
- Add `test_submit_command_forwards_caller_supplied_command_id` to [`tests/test_trading_inbound.py`](tests/test_trading_inbound.py) pinning that `TradingInbound` forwards and returns a supplied `command_id`; update `_FakeExecutionManager.submit_command` to mirror the real `command_id` parameter so the test double can no longer drift ahead of the production signature
- Update the `submit_command` fake in [`tests/test_launcher_execution_params_shim.py`](tests/test_launcher_execution_params_shim.py) to echo the supplied `command_id` instead of returning a fixed id: now that the launcher wrapper declares `command_id` explicitly, `PraxisOutbound` classifies the id as authoritative and rejects a returned id that diverges from the transmitted one, so the double must honour the identity contract the real execution layer upholds

## v0.81.0 on 15th of June, 2026

### BREAKING

- Cut the price/market-data path over to the Furnace Conduit data plane and drop the in-process market-data cache. The launcher no longer fetches or aggregates bars from Binance: predictions are consumed from the `furnace_conduit` volume (`/opt/conduit`) and ENTER / mark-to-market prices from the control-plane Arrow volume (`/opt/arrow`) via [`ArrowPriceStore`](praxis/arrow_price_store.py). The deployment must mount both volumes read-only on the praxis service. Pairs with Nexus v0.63.0 (the Conduit signal API): `PredictLoop` is constructed with `signal_bindings` + `conduit_dir` / `arrow_dir` (no `market_data_provider`), and the strategy manifest's per-strategy block is now `signal: {series, interval_seconds, ...}` (was `sensors: [...]`)

### NOTE

- Mark-price / fallback pricing resolves a single `(series, interval_seconds)` pair against the manifest via [`_resolve_mark_price_series`](praxis/launcher.py): the lone manifest series by default, or `PRAXIS_MARK_PRICE_SERIES` (plus `PRAXIS_MARK_PRICE_INTERVAL_SECONDS` when the chosen series is not declared by any strategy) to disambiguate; it raises loudly on an ambiguous (multiple) or empty series set. Conduit / Arrow roots are overridable via `PRAXIS_CONDUIT_DIR` / `PRAXIS_ARROW_DIR`

### Add

- Add [`praxis/arrow_price_store.py`](praxis/arrow_price_store.py) `ArrowPriceStore`: a stateless reader of `/opt/arrow/<series>/latest.arrow` returning the latest closed bar's `close` (`ts + interval_seconds <= now`, excluding the still-forming bar) as a `Decimal`; `None` on an absent frame, no closed bar, or a non-finite close. Backs `fallback_price_provider` (ENTER reference price) and the `MtmLoop` mark-price provider
- Add [`_resolve_mark_price_series`](praxis/launcher.py) and [`tests/test_resolve_mark_price_series.py`](tests/test_resolve_mark_price_series.py) (5 cases): single manifest series by default; `PRAXIS_MARK_PRICE_SERIES` override matching a manifest series; an off-manifest series with `PRAXIS_MARK_PRICE_INTERVAL_SECONDS`; and `RuntimeError`s on the multiple-series-unset and unknown-series-missing-interval paths

### Update

- Bump [`vaquum-nexus`](pyproject.toml) pin to v0.63.0 (`742d851`): `StartupSequencer` exposes `signal_bindings`, and `PredictLoop(signal_bindings, conduit_dir, arrow_dir)` polls Conduit instead of running in-process Limen inference

### Remove

- Remove [`praxis/market_data_cache.py`](praxis/market_data_cache.py) (`MainCache` / `CacheScheduler`) and [`praxis/market_data_poller.py`](praxis/market_data_poller.py) (`MarketDataPoller`), the launcher's `_start_poller` bootstrap, `_register_wired_kline_sizes`, and `_last_close_from_poller`; and the `vaquum_limen`, `binancial`, and `pandas` dependencies (`pandas` was used only by the removed market-data modules)

## v0.82.0 on 16th of June, 2026

### Add

- Add `TestLauncherFeeRateWiring` to [`tests/test_launcher.py`](tests/test_launcher.py) asserting the launcher wires the outcome translator with `_DEFAULT_FEE_RATE` (and that the rate is positive)
- Add TD-095 to [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md): the outcome translator re-derives `actual_fees` from the constant `_DEFAULT_FEE_RATE` rather than the venue-reported `FillReceived.fee` — exact for binsim's flat 10 bps, an approximation on a venue with a maker/taker split or fee discounts

### Fix

- Construct [`OutcomeTranslator`](praxis/outcome_translator.py) with `fee_rate=_DEFAULT_FEE_RATE` in the launcher instead of the zero-fee default: settled-fill `actual_fees` forwarded to Nexus now match the venue charge and the reservation-time fee estimate (both already use `_DEFAULT_FEE_RATE` = 0.10%), so realized PnL and capital release account for fees instead of treating trading as fee-free. The previous zero default was a testnet-era assumption that no longer holds against a fee-charging venue. Resolves TD-030; with `actual_fees` non-zero, Nexus's existing `realized_pnl = gross_pnl - actual_fees` (TD-051) takes effect

### Remove

- Prune the technical-debt register in [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md): collapse code-verified-resolved (TD-009, TD-020, TD-030, TD-045) and Conduit-obsoleted (TD-019, TD-060, TD-061, TD-062) entries to heading-only status markers, and reframe TD-053 around the Conduit/Arrow feed-staleness path that replaced `MarketDataPoller`

## v0.83.0 on 16th of June, 2026

### Add

- Add `TestTradeClosedPositionSemantics` to [`tests/test_execution_manager.py`](tests/test_execution_manager.py): an entry fill keeps the position and emits no `TradeClosed`; a reducing fill that closes the position emits `TradeClosed` and clears it. Update `test_full_cycle_submit_fill_outcome_shutdown` to assert the entry position survives a `FILLED` outcome (was asserting deletion)
- Add TD-096 to [`docs/TechnicalDebt.md`](docs/TechnicalDebt.md) recording that close-detection is side-based, not full-close-aware (partial-exit refinement for future scale-out strategies)

### Fix

- Emit `TradeClosed` only when a fill closes the position, not on every terminal filled order. `_build_outcome` and `_build_abort_outcome` now gate `TradeClosed` on [`_closes_position`](praxis/core/execution_manager.py) (a reducing fill — side opposite the open position), so an entry fill no longer emits it. Previously an entry's `TradeClosed` deleted its own position on event replay, so a process restart rebuilt zero open positions and boot reconciliation evicted the live position (orphaning the venue holding). Entry positions now survive a restart, which the Nexus boot reconciliation depends on

### Update

- Bump the [`vaquum-nexus`](pyproject.toml) pin to v0.64.0 (`5c63468`), which keys boot reconciliation on `pos.trade_id` and preserves + fails closed on a Nexus-only position instead of silently evicting it. The two changes are coupled: this PR keeps Praxis from under-reporting open positions on replay, and the Nexus pin keeps reconciliation from deleting them

## v0.83.1 on 16th of June, 2026

### Update

- Bump the [`vaquum-nexus`](pyproject.toml) pin to v0.65.0 (`f0dec50`): snaps sub-ULP Decimal residues in the capital aggregates to zero so a restart cannot brick recovery on a persisted `-1E-27` `working_order_notional`. Required alongside the fee-aware settlement (v0.82.0), which made the residue non-zero; full suite passes against the upgraded pin

## v0.84.0 on 18th of June, 2026

### Add
