- Correct three more. A shutdown send is passed over for an ordinary failure, an interrupt going straight past that catch. A position a fill meant to close whole but filled short of loses the rest too, the remainder moving to the account's dust, so it can lose more than the fill filled. And the sign turned for a short position is turned before the exit's fees come off, which come off either way round

- Make the renumbering count a blocking check as well. An ordinal standing in for a thing reads its meaning off a count stated earlier, and three rounds running moved that count as each pass turned up another branch. An ordinal qualifying a noun says what it counts and is left alone

- Correct five more. Of the five skipped stages only the platform ceilings and the money to set aside read the order's size; the book is read against a reference price and a per-strategy cap. The fee settlement goes onto the reserve rather than off it, growing it where a fill cost less than estimated. An expiry lowers what a position has pending too, there being no handling of its own for one. And stop counting the endings that leave a position alone: every count written so far has gone stale by the next round, two of them being the ones that turn an outcome back before any handling at all

- Correct four more. A fee shortfall refusing the move and the reserve taking the difference are two branches, not one sequence. An entry fill's raise does not end anything: the loop that handed the outcome over logs it and tells the strategy of the outcome regardless, so the position is left unwritten with nothing downstream the wiser. An exit's refusal comes before the position is changed rather than before it is read. And what the account and the strategy hold does not cap a release: a release past either is refused, and only one over by no more than a rounding margin is trimmed

- Make the rationale defect a blocking check, four verify rounds having found it in a different article each time. What the instances share is a purpose clause carrying an obligation — a word that gives a reason, then one that gives an obligation — which is the writer explaining rather than reporting, code holding conditions and not duties. A bare cause stays allowed, being a fact about the path. Proved by putting the shutdown's unreachable venue back and watching the gate refuse it

- Strip six rationales the code does not carry. The five skipped stages do not read only the account — three of them read the order's size — and which actions skip them is decided by the action alone, with nothing weighed about the decision. A shutdown send is passed over for raising anything at all, not for a venue being unreachable. An exit fill takes off what is pending down to nothing rather than always its own size. And the endings that leave a position alone are four, not three

- Open the next Nexus package with the exit, which the re-seed named first because two articles already written point at it. Tracing it turned up nine facts; four promoted, and two of those are the subjects the re-seed had queued already and stay queued. What an exit outcome changes is the article: the size coming off the position and off what it has pending, the three endings that leave the position alone and the fourth that reduces it with no money moved, and the two figures an exit is worth — the profit measured off the entry price and the money given back measured off the cost basis, capped at what the account and the strategy hold

- Correct three more, each a gloss the code does not carry. The five skipped stages do not all ask whether more should be committed: they read a drawdown, a book, a health snapshot, platform ceilings and the money to set aside, and nothing on a change's road checks that it keeps the size it re-aims. A refusal under the wrong stage's name does not travel out misattributed, the raise stopping it going out at all. And an exit fill naming no trade, or none with a position, raises alongside the two endings already named

- Say what turns an exit's fill back before it reaches the position: a release worked out and then refused, or a fill bigger than the position is. Scope the entry's own account to the entry, the exit's handling being the next topic rather than half an account here

- Correct two more. A cancellation is named in the walk's skipping set and a strategy never asks for one — the four things it asks for are to enter, to exit, to change and to abandon — so that entry is as dead as the abandonment's, and both now sit with the roads around the checks. And money is spent at the send only where the answer carried a reservation, which an entry's does and an exit's and a change's do not

- Split the roads around the checks into their own article. Two kinds of order reach the venue without the walk: an abandonment a strategy asks for, sent before the walk is reached, and an exit a stopping host sends, which writes its own allowed answer rather than asking for one. The walk's skipping set names an abandonment as though one could arrive, and nothing builds a request carrying one, so that entry decides nothing. Also say that no outcome but a fill, a refusal or a cancellation reaches a position

- Correct two more. An exit's fill reduces the position wherever no release was worked out for it at all, so the reduction waits on a release only when one was worked out and then refused. And the two figures re-averaged are not re-averaged alike: the entry price weights a price by a size, where the cost basis adds a value and a fee that are amounts already

- Correct three more claims a second reviewer found. Intake is not the one stage no action skips: an abandonment is named in the skipping set with the exits and cancellations, and nothing reaches the walk carrying one, the strategy's leaving by an earlier road. An exit's fill reduces the position only where the money it releases came back. And the two figures a fill re-averages each take a different number off it, the entry price the price it went off at and the cost basis the value it came to, which nothing requires to be the one multiplied by the size

- Re-seed the Nexus package from what its own tracing turned up: what an exit outcome changes, where a position's last scraps go, a deposit the venue reports and the pool it leaves alone, where a realized result is written down, stranded money found at boot, and money set aside that nobody claimed. The exit comes first, two articles already written saying a figure waits for it

- Say that an entry's position is looked at only once the money has moved. The two faults a fill can raise sit behind that move, so a fill the money would not move for comes back as a failure and never reaches them

- Correct five claims the verify stage found across four Nexus articles. A strategy's abandonment is sent without being walked through the checks at all, so the account of the walk could not open by saying every decision goes through it. A fee shortfall refuses a fill's move only once it passes the reserve by more than a rounding margin. A refusal or a cancellation of an exit lowers what a position has pending, so fills are not the only outcomes that reach one. And the two figures a fill re-averages are read for different things: the profit an exit reports is measured from the entry price, while the cost basis sets the money that exit gives back

- Take topic five through the same five stages. Tracing what Praxis sends back about a command turned up eleven facts, which the one-sentence test folded into one topic and the word cap then split in two: what an entry outcome changes, which is where the money set aside for a command goes as each kind of report arrives, and when an entry's position grows, which is the averaging a second fill does to the price and the cost basis a later exit is measured against

- Correct two Nexus articles a second reviewer dissented on. A change takes no new reservation because the capital stage never runs for it, and what covers it is what the command it re-aims already holds — the money that command went out on was spent at the send, so no reservation of its own still stands. And the two unreadable answers do not share one reason: an answer that is not a decision could pass unjudged, while one refusing under another stage's name is already a refusal and goes wrong only in whom it blames

- Read the list of checks the wiring tests cover off the gate itself rather than keeping it by hand. Three checks had been added to the gate without being added to the list, so nothing held them to reaching the report

- Fix what the review state meant, because it was unsatisfiable. It had been set to mean a verify pass that returned nothing, which an asked reviewer almost never does — the pass over three articles returned thirty-two judgements of supported against a handful of corrections and still left the count at zero. Reviewed now means the re-derivation happened and what it turned up was settled, by a fix or by a refusal recorded on the register page with its reason. It is also a fact about a version: a row reading reviewed names the version it covers in the article's own metadata, and the check refuses the claim where that stamp is missing or older than the article's last change, so editing a reviewed article un-reviews it with nobody having to judge that. On those terms one row was reviewed at first — two of the three a pass had settled were reopened by a second reviewer the same day — and the rest were not

- Take topic four through the same five stages. Tracing harvested six behaviours as H-076 to H-081 and the merge test folded all of them into one row, splitting nothing: a report matching no strategy yet is set aside under its command's name, every later report for that command queues behind it so the order they arrived in survives, only the head is tried, and a head still unmatched past the last wait has the whole queue dropped and logged rather than reported — so a strategy that never resolves costs its command every report, not only the first. Verify then found that a sibling counts as taken even where handing it over failed, so the queue advances regardless, and that a refusal identifies the stopping check to its caller rather than to the strategy — [an outcome waiting on a strategy](docs/wiki/atoms/an-outcome-waiting-on-a-strategy.md) says both

- Take the first Nexus topic through all five stages, which changed what got written. Tracing harvested five behaviours as H-071 to H-075; the merge test joined two, promoted two, and split the fifth out to the queue because how an abort reaches Praxis is an additional independent question — it goes by a road that never uses the walk's answer. Verify then found that a change shares the bypass while reducing nothing, that the trace had missed the shape a stage's answer must take, and that a stage which raises returns no decision at all, so the reservation is neither handed on nor released. Those additions took the article to 453 words against a 300-word cap, so it split: [the checks a decision passes](docs/wiki/atoms/the-checks-a-decision-passes.md) keeps the configured order, the skipping and what a decision carries out, and [what a check must answer with](docs/wiki/atoms/what-a-check-must-answer-with.md) takes the two shapes an answer may have and the two the walk declines to read. Topic four is traced and harvested as H-076 to H-081

- Run the Seed stage for the first Nexus package, which the two articles already written had skipped. The brief puts five stages in order — topics proposed and a reviewer challenging what is missing, then tracing with every behaviour harvested, then the merge test, then writing and verifying, then re-seeding — and those two were written straight off a candidate list that came out of a Praxis coverage review. The register now records the package's scope and that breach rather than tidying it away. The Seed approved five topics in a tracing order, kept both written ones with their scope, added the subject the candidate list missed — an outcome that cannot yet be matched to a strategy is parked and later outcomes for that command queue behind it — and queued seven more necessary Nexus subjects with what proves each is behaviour, so they enter a later package rather than enlarging this one

- Make a row's review state a state rather than decoration. The status column every register row carries was never read by the check, so `written` meant nothing and an unreviewed article was indistinguishable from a reviewed one. A row is now `written` or `reviewed` and nothing else — reviewed meaning a reviewer re-derived the article from the source its claims cite, which is the bar [issue #185](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/185) sets — the check refuses any other word, and the register states how many are each with the count checked against the table. Every row read `written` when the state was introduced: none had been through that re-derivation as a recorded step, and the number now says so on the page rather than being an absence nobody sees

- Correct both Nexus articles, which a review found wrong. Money is set aside partway through the staged checks rather than before them, so a later stage can refuse a decision that already holds capital — which is what the release on that path is for. Free capital is the pool less positions, working orders, money in flight, the fee reserve and existing reservations, not the two the article named. The first of the four limits weighs the order's worth without its fees, where the others count both. And the account of a timed-out send was wrong at its centre: the reservation is claimed before the command is handed over, so that money is already in flight and no expiry can release it — the registration taken beforehand owns the undoing, is allowed to fail, and has no release behind it

- Open the Nexus side, which [issue #185](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/185) puts in scope alongside Praxis, with the two articles a Praxis reader most needs. [How Nexus sets money aside](docs/wiki/atoms/how-nexus-sets-money-aside.md): a trade is weighed against its share of the pool, its strategy's budget, the free capital and a ceiling of four fifths deployed, and what is held covers the fees as well as the order. [What Nexus does when a command does not get across](docs/wiki/atoms/what-nexus-does-when-a-command-does-not-get-across.md): the money comes back on every road but one — a send that times out keeps it and marks the registration unknown, because the command may have arrived, and freeing it would let another trade spend money against an order that may be resting at the venue. Row identifiers widened to carry an `N` series

- Close the last branch the Praxis remainder carried: a ladder's price change is turned away before any of it starts by another change already under way on that ladder, by the ladder being under any hold, and by settings belonging to another way of working. The other three branches on that list — the not-active refusal on a protection re-price, the watch returning before its deadline, and squaring an order making no filled ending of its own — were written into their articles earlier in this release, and the list now says so

- Correct what a change to a part-shown order may name. The route said it may name only the amount to show and keeps its price; it may name the amount, the price, or both, and whichever it leaves out keeps the value it had. The refusal for settings matching no way of working is also two refusals, the first a raise before the change is queued and the second on the worker, and the route now distinguishes them

- Close out the five routes against a third reading of their evidence, all fifty-two claims of which resolve. A lost protection no longer reads as closing the position: only one of the two policies attempts a closing order, and even that is an attempt. The hold that sets a change aside covers a poisoned account as well as one catching up, and that one does not lift while the host runs. A worker is stopped as its own account is unregistered rather than past them all. The retained backfill hold belongs to one adapter branch, and incomplete is the backfill's result rather than the squaring's. Starting a dequeued request now names the way of working that owns it — a run, a ladder, a part-shown order, a bracket — instead of pointing every one at plain placement. The bounded shutdown wait and unregistration moved to the article that owns them

- Add the last two Praxis subjects the register carried as seeded. [When a reconnection releases an account](docs/wiki/atoms/when-a-reconnection-releases-an-account.md): the phase an account is in is the one thing recorded and the gate on its work is read off it, a feed going down mid-asking marks that pass to end gated, a reconnection arriving mid-asking books exactly one rerun, and only a pass that got all the way through releases it — the round of checks that runs on a timer never asks. [When an older record can be opened](docs/wiki/atoms/when-an-older-record-can-be-opened.md): a shape from a newer build is refused outright, an identity another build wrote is refused however right the shape, and old duplicate marks come forward only where every one matches a fill whose pair is known

- Add [How an outside event enters the record](docs/wiki/atoms/how-an-outside-event-enters-the-record.md), the first of the Praxis subjects the register carried as seeded. News from outside is written by the account's own writer and nobody else: a caller queues the event with a place for the answer, wakes the writer, and waits on what the writer did. An account still starting has no writer draining that queue, so the caller doing the starting writes straight through — and only that caller. Poisoning is weighed again after the record is written, so a write can land and the caller still be told it failed, and a writer called off mid-drain leaves that caller waiting on an answer that never comes

- Tighten what the five routes cite and what they leave out. The cancel route gained the step its own evidence already proved and its body did not — a request whose trade cannot be named is left without a report, past the venue call and past any write it made. The stop route gained the stages it had compressed away: the wait for open work to clear, the feeds and the venue connection closing, the accounts being unregistered, and the failure there that is kept while the rest are attempted and raised past them, leaving trading marked as started. Entry citations now carry the handoff from the host through to the manager rather than stopping at the guard, a confirmed cancellation is distinguished from a refused one in the claim about what is written first, and answering an orphan command is named as the report it sends rather than the classification it makes

- Correct what reviewing the five spines under that new bar turned up. The change route had the stop-price, weighted-field and buy-exposure refusals as late arms on the worker; all three run before the change is queued, and the route now says so. The start route split on a live venue against a simulated one; the branch is the adapter's kind, and the simulated venue uses the same adapter, so it takes the feed and backfill path too. One claim said exactly one request leaves the ordinary queue each pass, where an empty queue ends the pass having taken none. The stop flag was cited at the handlers that install it rather than the wait and the two setters, and the second shutdown pass was described as following the abandonments rather than beginning without waiting for them. The scan the squaring tick asks for carries drains, changes and failed closes besides protection, and now links accordingly

- Give a spine evidence of its own, and check it the way an article's is checked. An article owns a behaviour; none owns the order behaviours happen in, which is the whole of what a spine adds — so every ordering claim a spine made was borrowing an article that did not support it, which two review rounds kept finding and no rule could catch. A spine now carries claim and source pairs proving its route against the pinned commits, resolved on the same terms: the lines must exist, hold code rather than a docstring or a log call, and sit at the declared baseline. Row identifiers, the register's row table, and the index's link pattern all widened to cover spines, and the voice rules now apply to them

- Add the four remaining spines the brief names: [draining an account's queues](docs/wiki/spines/draining-an-accounts-queues.md), the pass every other route is carried by; [changing work already sent](docs/wiki/spines/changing-work-already-sent.md), with one arm per way of working; [stopping a host with work in flight](docs/wiki/spines/stopping-a-host-with-work-in-flight.md), which abandons what is in flight, cancels what is resting, and carries on through three things going wrong; and [starting a host back up](docs/wiki/spines/starting-a-host-back-up.md), which ends either ready or gated. Each was walked against the code rather than assembled from the articles, and each sentence names the article that owns it

- Add the first spine, [Cancelling a trade](docs/wiki/spines/cancelling-a-trade.md), and the check that makes a spine one. [Issue #185](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/185) names two kinds of page and calls the spine the deliverable — a numbered walkthrough sequencing articles end to end — and names one published cancel spine with its failure forks as the guard against shipping hundreds of correct short pages that still leave a cancel unfollowable outside `execution_manager.py`. The directory for them existed and was empty, and nothing checked for one. The route runs from the asking to the report in fifteen steps, grouped the way the code branches rather than as one line: the refusals before anything is queued, the three arms a queued cancellation takes — work being fed out, no order ever sent, an order resting — the three results of the one venue call, and what the caller is told on each. A first draft sequenced the articles instead of the dispatch, which read as a single fourteen-step line and put alternative arms in series; both reviewers walked it against the code and rejected it, which is the bar the issue sets for a spine. A spine asserts nothing of its own: the check refuses a step that names no article, a route numbered with a gap, and a page under `spines/` with no route at all. A step ends at the next step or the next heading, underlined headings included, so a link below one cannot stand in for the step's own; and a bullet saying where the route is not taken names an article on the same terms, since that is a claim about the route too. Ownership is also per sentence rather than per step: one link at the end of a step reads as covering every sentence in it, which is how a spine comes to assert what no article says — the sentence carrying the claim is not the sentence carrying the link. A trailing run of links and a bold label assert nothing and need no owner. Every one of these holes was found by walking the spine rather than by the check, and each now has a test that fails when the rule is removed

- Add [What the host refuses first](docs/wiki/atoms/what-the-host-refuses-first.md). Placing a request, cancelling one and changing one share one guard that runs before the books or the worker are reached: a host shutting down refuses all three, and each then requires the named account's own start to have finished. A caller turned away there is told by a raise with nothing written down — and because ready is set only after a start finishes, work arriving that way never belongs to an account still starting, which is what the startup waits further in are for. Written because the cancel spine asserted these refusals with no article owning them

- Add six articles to [the wiki](docs/wiki/README.md): what the simulated venue turns an order away for, and what it turns a depth snapshot away for; what a lost protection costs the account, freeze by freeze; what stops the order that closes the position it leaves open — a sell for a long, a buy for a short — and how big that order goes out; and the holds a reported finding places on an account, down to the lifts nothing in the running system calls Add a seventh on the part of a fill a command counts: the whole fill reaches the order and the money record, and only what fits the order's budget reaches the figures Nexus hears, so the two part company by exactly what was discarded. Add an eighth on what a restart puts back: each kind of live work rebuilt at the point it had reached, with nothing already standing at the venue sent again, and a scheme's hold rebuilt under the precedence the running one keeps. Add the rest of the Praxis side: how a ladder's prices are changed and what can leave the new grid short; how protection's prices are changed, and the endings where nothing replaces the pair; how a schedule change is carried out, the parts already sent kept and the next set a full gap from now; what startup does with orphaned work on both sides of the record; the window a movement is found in; a weight curve that plans a part of nothing; when a venue call is sent again and what each attempt costs; how an unfinished close is recovered; how an order is brought back into step with the venue; and what a restart puts back of a bracket. 703 articles, 11,326 line references across 5,080 claims

- Add the branches three articles stopped short of. A venue frame whose handler raises is logged and the frame dropped, so a feed being up says nothing about whether what arrived was taken in. A change to a buy that would commit more money than the order first did is refused, and the caller told to place a new order. Each leg of a protective pair takes exactly one of an outright price or a distance from the entry, and both or neither is refused

### Fix

- Name the condition on when Praxis claims the money set aside for a command. The article on what becomes of that money said the claim is made before the order goes, which holds only where the receiving side was taken as bound to the identifier it was sent; where it was not, the order goes first and the claim follows

- Qualify four recovery guarantees the articles stated as settled and the code leaves open. An unknown protective pair past its deadline is not always remediated: a leg query that fails, or fills that will not settle, return before the deadline is reached. A restart waiting for a filled entry before putting protection up is not waiting for exposure to exist — a part-filled order already shows in the holdings. An amended pair passed over on resume is not handed to the watch, which walks only rebuilt brackets. And a close recovered from the venue's fills takes them only where the record carries none for it yet

- Correct ten claims in the new articles that misread the code they cite. A replacement protective pair the venue cannot be asked about was described as protection lost, when only a pair the venue returns marked rejected is — anything unanswerable leaves it unknown. A stop between cancelling a single order and placing its replacement was described as leaving the old order resting, when by then it is gone and nothing stands in its place. A schedule change was described as always replanning, when only a change to the count does. A ladder amend's clean write-off was described as the road a cancellation failure takes, when a cancel is already committed by then. Also: the holding and the reported amount part company over a buy's commission as well as over the budget, the venue sweep is per symbol and counts closed orders as known, a movement in another asset carries the mark forward without a handover, and a resumed ladder needs more than its surviving rungs settling to finish

- Finish three articles that covered a path and left out a branch a reader needs. [When a send's outcome is unknown](docs/wiki/atoms/when-a-send-is-unclear.md) now carries the fill that arrives after the send was written off as refused: it is taken into the closed order, the ending already reported stands, and the name cannot be used again. [How a price change is carried out](docs/wiki/atoms/how-a-price-change-is-carried-out.md) now says what a restart keeps of a change — the count the next name is built from, and nothing else, the change itself living in memory alone. Two further subjects outgrew their parents and became articles of their own: the window a movement is found in, which never reaches back past the moment the account was taken on, and a weight curve that plans a part of nothing, caught on the way out rather than at the start

- Count a figure in the wiki's own prose that a line wrap separated from the thing it counts. Every count the gate checks was matched with a literal space, so the index's article count sat wrong across a wrap while the check passed. Every count the gate reads now matches across any whitespace, and a term another article owns is matched as whole words with the same tolerance — so `order booked` no longer reads as the order book, and a term wrapped onto the next line no longer escapes needing its link

- State the finite, positive requirement a protective pair's figures are held to, and the refusal of a change raising a buy's commitment, in the prose of the articles that cite them rather than in their front matter alone

### Change

- State the wiki's scope as both repositories, which is what [issue #185](https://github.com/Vaquum/Praxis/issues/185) asks for. The index and the register had come to say the wiki was about Praxis and that Nexus was not in the package — a narrowing that was never asked for and does not match the brief, which puts Nexus's 81 files in scope and requires boundary articles written from both ends. Seed the seven Nexus subjects a reader of the Praxis side is left needing, as candidates for their own Seed stage

- Name six Nexus modules in [the ledger](docs/wiki/ledger.md) with a row of their own rather than a passing citation, taking the modules it names from 17 to 21 of the 70: the two reactions a reported finding runs into, the two lists of answers they choose from, and the mode controller and manifest those answers reach. Praxis stands at 46 `full`, 38 `partial` and 4 `uncovered`, `bracket_params.py` having moved off the uncovered list
